### [So You Want to Build Your Own Data Center](<https://blog.railway.com/p/data-center-build-part-one>)

*blog.railway.com · 2026-09-23 · engineering*

Railway describes why it moved its compute off Google Cloud Platform and began building its own physical infrastructure through a Railway Metal project. The post covers its colocation choice (a private cage), how power density and per-rack redundant feeds drove server selection and design, and the timeline from project start to first servers and user access.

- **Technical:** Railway chose cage colocation over greenfield buildout or rack colocation, and power, which drives cooling costs, was the largest expense.
- **Technical:** Server and CPU selection was driven mainly by power density, and each rack needs two fully independent power feeds designed to survive loss of one.
- **Business:** Railway left its hyperscaler to address pricing limits (including egress fees), limited service levels, and weak upstream support despite multi-million dollar annual spend.
- **Business:** Power is billed as a fixed monthly commit regardless of consumption, and $/kW rates vary widely by geography.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://blog.railway.com/p/data-center-build-part-one>)

### [SOM designs "vertical data center" for Downtown Kansas City](<https://www.dezeen.com/2026/08/05/data-center-skyscraper-kansas-city-som/>)

*dezeen.com · 2026-08-06 · news*

Architecture studio SOM has released designs for a 20-storey vertical data center at 934 Central Street in downtown Kansas City, developed by Revitalization Unlimited. The tower would stack data floors, cooling and power generation on a 0.23-acre site currently occupied by the Western Newspaper Union Building. Local residents have pushed back over the project's speculative nature, urban heat island concerns and the building's demolition, and the City Planning Commission was scheduled to review it on 5 August 2026.

- **Technical:** The design stacks data systems, cooling and power generation vertically within one tower rather than across a horizontal campus footprint.
- **Technical:** On-site power is reportedly generated from natural gas cells, with the diagram stating the system is 100 per cent independent from the grid.
- **Technical:** Water is internally cycled through equipment on upper floors, with evaporative cooling elements shown at the bulkhead.
- **Business:** The building is designed to convert to residential, office or mixed-use space if computing demand declines.
- **Business:** Community opposition centers on the project's speculative nature, urban heat island effects and demolition of an existing building.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://www.dezeen.com/2026/08/05/data-center-skyscraper-kansas-city-som/>)

### [Five Ways Nvidia Co-Design Maximizes the Value of Every Byte in the AI Factory](<https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/five-ways-nvidia-co-design-maximizes-the-value-of-every-byte-in-the-ai-factory/>)

*developer.nvidia.com · 2026-11-05 · engineering*

NVIDIA's developer blog describes five co-design techniques for getting more useful work from each byte of GPU memory in AI factories. The excerpt covers NVFP4 low-precision formats, NVLink scale-up domains with wide expert parallelism for MoE models, and benchmark claims for Vera Rubin NVL72 versus GB300 NVL72.

- **Technical:** NVFP4 uses a 4-bit E2M1 element with FP8 micro-block scaling and a per-tensor FP32 scale, reducing model footprint by about 3.5x versus FP16 and 1.8x versus FP8.
- **Technical:** NVFP4 KV cache cuts cache footprint by up to 50% versus FP8, keeping roughly 2x more context resident on device.
- **Technical:** Wide expert parallelism in TensorRT-LLM spreads MoE experts across eight or more GPUs, reducing expert weights per GPU and increasing capacity for KV cache and batches.
- **Business:** NVIDIA reports up to 30x throughput per megawatt for Vera Rubin NVL72 versus GB300 NVL72 on SemiAnalysis AgentX at 160 tokens per second per user.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/five-ways-nvidia-co-design-maximizes-the-value-of-every-byte-in-the-ai-factory/>)

### [Formalizing Nvidia PTX in Lean to Prove GPU Kernel Correctness](<https://sf-tensor.com/engineering/formalizing-ptx>)

*sf-tensor.com · 2026-11-03 · research*

The article describes an approach to formally proving GPU kernel correctness by formalizing the PTX semantics in Lean, with an LLM writing proofs that Lean's small trusted checker verifies. It explains the PTX memory model, including program order, reads-from, coherence order, synchronizes-with and causality, and reports proofs for Hopper GEMM, FlashAttention, a Mixture-of-Experts pipeline and a megakernel.

- **Technical:** Formal proofs in Lean aim to catch subtle bugs such as race conditions that testing cannot cover, since tests only check a finite number of cases.
- **Technical:** The approach works at the PTX level because PTX is what CUDA programs compile to and because it has a precisely specified memory consistency model.
- **Technical:** The new approach supports data-dependent control flow, TMA, async operations, acquire-release synchronization, proxy fences, and megakernels, which the earlier work did not.
- **Business:** Proofs are only as strong as the formalization of PTX, so correctness claims hold within the scope of that formalization.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://sf-tensor.com/engineering/formalizing-ptx>)

### [Optimizing 124B MoE inference on a single Nvidia DGX Spark](<https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/3-ways-to-boost-moe-inference-on-nvidia-dgx-spark/>)

*developer.nvidia.com · 2026-11-02 · engineering*

NVIDIA's developer blog describes three techniques for speeding up inference of the Ling-3.0-flash mixture-of-experts model on a single DGX Spark: Humming MXFP4 MoE kernels, online FP8 quantization of the LM head, and multi-token prediction speculative decoding with NEXTN. The post reports that decoding was memory-bound and notes an integration issue that can make the combined configuration slower than baseline.

- **Technical:** Profiling showed batch-size-1 decoding on DGX Spark was memory-bound, with reading model weights driving execution time.
- **Technical:** Online FP8 quantization of the LM head halves the weight-read volume for that layer, which is a high-value target under a bandwidth bottleneck.
- **Technical:** Humming compiles kernels at runtime via NVRTC for specific shapes, so individual kernels build in seconds versus tens of minutes for the FlashInfer CUTLASS path.
- **Business:** The post frames single-workstation inference optimization as a way to generate tokens faster on constrained hardware.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/3-ways-to-boost-moe-inference-on-nvidia-dgx-spark/>)

### [A History of Nvidia Stream Multiprocessor (2020)](<https://fabiensanglard.net/cuda/index.html>)

*fabiensanglard.net · 2026-11-01 · engineering*

A technical walkthrough of the evolution of Nvidia's stream multiprocessor (SM) architecture, from the 2006 unified Tesla G80 through Fermi, Kepler, Maxwell, Pascal and Turing. The author describes how the unified design replaced fixed-function vertex, fragment and geometry stages and explains SIMT warp execution, SFU units, register file and float64 limitations.

- **Technical:** The unified Tesla architecture replaced separate vertex, fragment and geometry stages with Stream Multiprocessors that run any kernel type, letting load balance shift automatically with the pipeline's needs.
- **Technical:** Warps of 32 threads execute in SIMT fashion, and performance depends on hiding memory latency with schedulable warps and avoiding control-flow divergence.
- **Technical:** The G80 had no hardware float64 support, so double-precision work was done in software with a large speed penalty.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://fabiensanglard.net/cuda/index.html>)

### [Only Accepted Tokens Count: An Energy Accounting of Speculative Decoding Under Latency Targets](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40287>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-29 · research*

An arXiv paper measures the energy cost of speculative decoding for LLM serving on H100 and H200 GPUs under SGLang with CUDA graphs, across batch sizes up to 64 and five corpora. It finds that energy savings depend on draft acceptance rate, shrink as batch size grows, and can become a loss at large batches with block drafters.

- **Technical:** On dense models, verification rather than drafting dominates the energy cost of speculative decoding.
- **Technical:** At large batch sizes, block drafters push verification into the compute-bound regime, where speculation's energy saving becomes a loss.
- **Business:** Meeting a tighter latency target with speculation can cost additional energy, which affects tokens served per unit of facility power.
- **Technical:** Larger batches lower energy per token without speculation, and speculation adds to that saving only when acceptance is high.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40287>)

This arXiv paper presents a cross-layer systematic review (PRISMA 2020 framework) of LLM serving in cloud datacenters, covering hardware virtualization, cluster scheduling, and runtime inference optimizations. It also evaluates serverless, checkpointing, and trusted execution environment architectures and outlines Pareto trade-offs among throughput, latency, cost, and confidentiality, along with a research roadmap.

- **Technical:** Serving large models faces bottlenecks including the HBM wall, the asymmetry between compute-bound prefilling and memory-bandwidth-bound decoding, and KV cache expansion.
- **Technical:** The review covers continuous batching, prefill-decoding disaggregation, and hierarchical KV cache offloading as runtime optimizations.
- **Business:** The paper formalizes multi-dimensional trade-offs among throughput, latency, monetary cost, and confidentiality for cloud LLM infrastructure.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33475>)

An arXiv paper estimates the embodied emissions of a chiplet-based GPU scaled with 2.5D and 3D packaging using the imec.netzero modelling platform. Doubling compute units and HBM capacity, the authors report an 89% EE increase for 3D scaling versus 141% for 2.5D scaling.

- **Technical:** 3D scaling of a chiplet-based GPU produced an 89% increase in embodied emissions when compute units and HBM capacity were doubled, compared with 141% for 2.5D scaling.

*arXiv · 2026-10-23 · research*

This arXiv paper examines distributed LLM training across heterogeneous clients and geo-distributed sites as an alternative to centralized GPU data centers. It proposes a unified architecture with a cloud-hosted parameter server, outbound gRPC connections, and shared communication, compression, and fault-tolerance components. The authors report experiments training GPT-2 Medium and Llama3-1B across multiple university sites and outline open problems.

- **Technical:** The proposed architecture uses a cloud-hosted parameter server that coordinates heterogeneous clients over outbound gRPC connections.
- **Technical:** One communication, compression, and fault-tolerance stack is reused for both pretraining and several fine-tuning methods.
- **Technical:** Real geo-distributed experiments on GPT-2 Medium and Llama3-1B pretraining reportedly showed stable convergence under realistic conditions.
- **Business:** Centralized LLM development is described as hard to sustain, while smaller institutions holding private data lack comparable infrastructure.

### [PlaneZip: Lossless Bit-Plane Compression of Training Activations Offloaded over PCIe](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25033>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-18 · research*

This arXiv paper presents PlaneZip, a lossless bit-plane compression scheme for GPU training activations that are swapped to host memory over PCIe. The authors report compression ratios and simulated reductions in rematerialization overhead relative to a bandwidth-aware planner and the MegTaiChi cost model.

- **Technical:** A per-128-byte-block codec selects the smallest of zero-value, delta bit-plane, bit-plane, and zero-masked bit-plane encodings to compress swapped activations.
- **Technical:** Compressing activations on the copy path lets the planner budget link time in compressed transfer times, changing when swapping beats recomputation.
- **Business:** Simulated results claim a 47% and 31% cut in rematerialization overhead versus a bandwidth-aware planner without compression, with an estimated engine area of 0.036 square millimeters.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25033>)

*dennysora.me · 2026-10-12 · engineering*

dgxtop is an Apache-2.0 Rust terminal monitor for NVIDIA GPUs on Linux, built for the DGX Spark. It shows per-GPU utilization, memory, temperature, power and clocks, per-process GPU memory use, and a persistent history of GPU jobs and machine metrics. The project page covers installation, supported platforms and the v2.1 release.

- **Technical:** Records every GPU process from start to end, including peak GPU memory, runtime and how it ended (exit code, signal, OOM-killer likely, or stopped from dgxtop).
- **Technical:** Requires the NVIDIA driver for GPU metrics; CPU, memory, disk and network monitoring work without it. Supports Linux x86\_64 and aarch64 with glibc 2.34 or newer, and WSL 2, but not WSL 1, musl, macOS or Windows.
- **Technical:** Installer verifies the release against SHA256SUMS and keeps the previous version, with a rollback option.

### [Democratizing MoE inference on commodity GPUs with CoMoE](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09424>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-07 · research*

The arXiv paper presents CoMoE, an MoE inference system for consumer GPUs that uses host-centric routing to reduce inter-GPU communication over PCIe without P2P support. The authors report up to 1.46x throughput improvement on RTX 5090 GPUs, approaching NVLink-capable A800 performance at 23.4% of the hardware cost.

- **Technical:** Host-backed token multicast writes shared tokens to the host once, eliminating redundant outbound transmission for token dispatch.
- **Technical:** Fine-grained token-level aggregation using host staging buffers replaces global synchronization and mitigates straggler effects during token combine.
- **Business:** The approach targets lower-cost MoE inference deployment, reporting performance near NVLink-capable A800 GPUs at 23.4% of hardware cost.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09424>)

### [Heddle: Learning Structural Templates for Parallelism Planning on Heterogeneous GPU Clusters](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34244>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-28 · research*

Heddle is a learning-based parallelism planner for training large machine learning models on heterogeneous GPU clusters. It reduces planning to searching over pipeline structural templates learned offline across cluster configurations.

- **Technical:** Planning is reduced to a search over pipeline structures, with remaining plan details derived by rule or from a small candidate set once structure is fixed.
- **Technical:** The planner is evaluated against five existing planners across clusters with varying GPU types and node sizes, with throughput gains of up to 84.5% on dense models and 4.6x on MoE models.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34244>)

### [EMA: Elastic and Performance Transparent Memory Across GPUs](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27040>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-22 · research*

EMA is an arXiv research paper presenting a memory sharing system that lets GPUs within a multi-GPU server borrow and reclaim memory from each other to form an elastic pool. The paper claims prefetching makes remote memory performance indistinguishable from local memory, and reports up to 52% higher individual user throughput and 96% of the throughput of a system with 2X capacity.

- **Technical:** EMA lets GPUs within a server borrow and reclaim memory from each other, addressing imbalanced memory demand such as LLM inference workloads.
- **Technical:** Prefetching is used to hide remote access costs so borrowers see remote and local memory as performance-equivalent.
- **Business:** Reported evaluation shows up to 52% higher individual user throughput and 96% of the throughput of a system provisioned with 2X capacity.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27040>)

Lumai argues that as AI inference splits into prefill and decode stages, the next step is to specialize hardware for each, since prefill is compute-bound and decode is memory-bandwidth-bound. - **Technical:** Longer context windows, retrieval-augmented generation, multimodal inputs and agentic workflows increase the context that must be processed before token generation.

### [Towards Training Private LLMs: Exploring Fine-Tuning Language Models on Apple Silicon with RDMA over Thunderbolt](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18066>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-16 · research*

This arXiv paper studies whether Apple Silicon Mac Studio nodes connected by RDMA-over-Thunderbolt can serve as a platform for private LLM fine-tuning. The authors measure RDMA-over-TB bandwidth, extend Apple's implementation with multi-trunk communication, persistent worker threads and gradient overlap, and report up to 1.6X weak-scaling throughput gains on a four-node cluster fine-tuning Qwen3-9B. The paper also compares the setup with an NVIDIA H100 platform on memory capacity, throughput and acquisition cost.

- **Technical:** Measured RDMA-over-Thunderbolt bandwidth on Mac Studio nodes is far below nominal Thunderbolt specifications.
- **Technical:** Multi-trunk communication, persistent worker threads and CPU-side gradient overlap improved weak-scaling throughput by up to 1.6X over the single-trunk, non-overlapped baseline.
- **Business:** The authors argue Apple Silicon can be a cost-effective option for private LLM fine-tuning compared with NVIDIA H100 platforms.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18066>)

### [(conf talk) Liberating the A100 beast inside Nvidia's CMP 170HX e-waste](<https://talks.mrmcd.net/2026/talk/ZDRJLV/>)

*talks.mrmcd.net · 2026-09-14 · research*

A conference talk describes unlocking the full 200 TFLOPS compute and 64GB HBM2e memory of Nvidia's CMP 170HX, a crypto-mining variant of the A100, by exploiting a signature-length overflow in the booter\_load Falcon microcode after cracking a weakly encrypted debug AES key. The speaker argues the restrictions were imposed by VBIOS firmware rather than fused hardware, and that the card is useful for LLM token generation despite its limited VRAM.

- **Technical:** The CMP 170HX limits were imposed by VBIOS firmware rather than permanent defects or fuses on the GPU die.
- **Technical:** The VBIOS Falcon microcode was encrypted with AES-ECB instead of AES-CBC, leaking a recurring pattern that helped reveal the test AES key.
- **Technical:** A missing length check on the signature in booter\_load allows an overflow that enables a ROP chain to set Privilege Level Masks and unlock compute and memory.
- **Business:** The end of Ethereum mining has turned the CMP 170HX into potential e-waste, and the talk presents a path to reuse it for AI workloads.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://talks.mrmcd.net/2026/talk/ZDRJLV/>)

### [Performance Characterization of SPEC CPU 2026 on AMD EPYC 9755 Processor](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01527>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-01 · research*

This arXiv paper presents a microarchitecture-level performance characterization of SPEC CPU 2026 on AMD EPYC 9755 processors based on Zen 5. It analyzes both SPECrate and SPECspeed suites across pipeline, control flow, cache, and instruction mix behavior, including scale effects that appear at full system utilization. The authors frame the results as groundwork for workload-driven design of next-generation datacenter processors.

- **Technical:** The multi-lens analysis identifies three behavioral clusters: frontend control-flow-dominated workloads, high-efficiency compute workloads that suffer SMT contention at scale, and memory bandwidth-bound workloads with poor L3 filtering even at single-copy.
- **Technical:** Scale-dependent effects such as SMT dispatch contention and L3 capacity interference emerge only at full system utilization and are not visible in conventional single-copy characterization.
- **Business:** The study is positioned as an empirical foundation for architectural research and design decisions for next-generation datacenter processors.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01527>)

The arXiv paper introduces ShardMeter, an analytical performance model that predicts end-to-end runtime of transformer training across sharded, multi-cluster, and decentralized configurations. It estimates per-GPU and per-island throughput, training cost, wall-clock time, and bottlenecks for a given model and hardware topology.

- **Technical:** The model identifies diminishing-return regimes as island size increases and quantifies transitions between compute-bound and communication-bound scaling.
- **Business:** The model models cost-throughput trade-offs for large-scale decentralized training, aimed at selecting deployment plans and avoiding costly trial and error.

### [TEMPO: Makespan-Aware Expert-Parallel Load Balancing Across Memory- and Compute-Bound Regimes](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13057>)

*arXiv · 2026-08-13 · research*

The paper studies load balancing for expert-parallel MoE serving across GPUs, arguing that expert cost is neither linear in token count nor in activated experts alone, and proposes a max-affine cost profile. It presents TEMPO, a makespan-aware dispatcher integrated with SGLang, reporting up to 15.5% gains where regimes mix and 4-6% throughput gains on Qwen3-235B end-to-end.

- **Technical:** Below roughly 156-168 tokens, HBM weight streaming dominates expert cost, so cost tracks activated replicas rather than tokens.
- **Technical:** Above that threshold, grouped GEMM rounds tokens to 128-tile M-tiles, so splitting an expert adds padded compute.
- **Technical:** TEMPO's SGLang integration fuses dispatch with count collection into one in-graph kernel and runs out-of-process.
- **Business:** End-to-end on Testbed B, Qwen3-235B gains 4-6% throughput and about 15.6% lower p99 latency, while DeepSeek-V3 shows only mechanism cost.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13057>)

### [Nvidia Exemplar Cloud: Lessons for Unlocking Performance on AI Infrastructure](<https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-exemplar-cloud-lessons-for-unlocking-full-performance-on-ai-infrastructure/>)

*developer.nvidia.com · 2026-08-12 · engineering*

NVIDIA describes debugging investigations into performance gaps of 8% to 12% between partner AI clusters built on H100, GB200 NVL72, or GB300 NVL72 systems and NVIDIA's reference architecture. The post walks through four case studies covering virtualization/SMMU, CPU power and NUMA placement, NCCL queue-pair concurrency on 1.6 Tbps fabrics, and hardware installation defects. It lists prerequisites and profiling tools such as Nsight Systems and nccl-tests.

- **Technical:** Configuration choices in the kernel, hypervisor, BIOS, and NCCL settings can each cost a few percent and compound into an 8% to 12% gap, enough to miss the 95% threshold for Exemplar Cloud validation.
- **Technical:** A GB200 NVL72 partner deployment running DeepSeek-V3 MoE FP8 pre-training inside a VM ran iterations 12% to 14% slower than bare metal, while dense Llama 3 70B recipes ran within 3% of reference.
- **Business:** Exemplar Cloud validation requires reaching 95% of the NVIDIA reference architecture performance, making these tuning gaps directly relevant to partner cluster acceptance.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-exemplar-cloud-lessons-for-unlocking-full-performance-on-ai-infrastructure/>)

### [Architectural Implications of Agentic AI Workflows](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04458>)

*arXiv · 2026-08-05 · research*

This arXiv paper characterizes the architectural demands of agentic AI workflows, based on a production study at Microsoft Azure and a controlled study of open-source frameworks. It argues that fragmented CPU-GPU execution strands capacity and calls for role-aware server designs, and it presents Agora, a prototype for commodity servers that harvests idle CPU cores and oversubscribes GPU memory.

- **Technical:** Agentic workflows repeatedly cross the CPU-GPU boundary, placing the CPU on the critical path because orchestration and tools run on the host.
- **Technical:** Fragmented execution strands CPU and GPU capacity despite bursty demand, and homogeneous CPU provisioning is inefficient for different software roles.
- **Technical:** The Agora prototype pools cores by role, uses affinity-aware scheduling, and prefetches agent state to hide GPU memory swap latency while preserving tail latency.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04458>)

### [An Optimal Power-Flow Grouping Topology for Single-Isolation-Stage Multi-Output Solid-State Transformers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09305>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-06 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes an interleaved submodule grouping topology for single-isolation-stage multi-output solid-state transformers, aimed at EV fast-charging and data center multi-output dc supply. The authors report reduced balancing power, lower dc-link voltage difference, and about 5.6% higher measured efficiency on a six-submodule prototype, with stable post-fault operation demonstrated.

- **Technical:** Interleaving submodules assigned to different output ports localizes power redistribution and reduces the power processed by balancing circuits compared with sequential grouping.
- **Technical:** A six-submodule prototype showed a 63% reduction in maximum submodule dc-link voltage difference and a 14.5% reduction in peak balancing current under highly unbalanced loading.
- **Business:** Eliminating the separate high-power dc/dc interface stage targets lower losses for multi-output power conversion in data centers and EV charging.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09305>)

### [Supporting Data Center Grid Integration via Co-Optimized Battery Energy Storage Sizing and Capacity Allocation](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09001>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-06 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes an optimization framework to size a battery energy storage system that helps large data centers meet grid interconnection requirements for demand flexibility, ramping limits, and ride-through. The model co-optimizes BESS power and energy capacity across demand response, fluctuation mitigation, and reactive voltage support, and a case study uses a real data center load profile.

- **Technical:** The framework co-optimizes BESS power rating and energy capacity while allocating capability across demand response, load-fluctuation mitigation, and voltage support at the point of common coupling.
- **Technical:** Minute-level scheduling enforces state-of-charge feasibility and load ramp constraints and uses a controllable queue of delay-insensitive computing tasks to add flexibility.
- **Business:** Grid requirements on flexibility, ramping, and ride-through can delay data center interconnection, and the paper aims to give guidance for interconnection planning and deployment.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09001>)

### [Grid-Oriented Probabilistic Load Forecasting for AI Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08929>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-06 · research*

This arXiv paper presents an open, grid-oriented benchmark for probabilistic load forecasting of AI data centers, using measured GPU power from a 448-GPU cluster. It evaluates forecasts across probabilistic prediction, online calibration, temporal scenario generation, and reserve sizing.

- **Technical:** A quantile LightGBM forecast reached only 63.4% coverage for a nominal 90% interval after a change in cluster utilization, while online regime-conditioned calibration raised coverage to 87.3%.
- **Technical:** Independent sampling produced unrealistic ramp distributions and excessive reserve procurement even with well-calibrated marginal forecasts.
- **Business:** Reconstructing temporal dependence from historical forecast errors lowered realized reserve cost by 7.4-10.6% across three forecasting tasks, suggesting marginal forecast quality alone is insufficient for operational decisions.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08929>)

### [Reducing Data-Center Load Transfers with Ride-Through-Aware Voltage Control](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08777>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-05 · research*

The paper proposes a ride-through-aware extension of quadratic voltage control to reduce data center transfers to backup supply during grid faults, which can abruptly reduce grid-served demand. It derives design requirements and objectives for reactive-power coordination and evaluates them on IEEE 14-bus, New England 39-bus, and NPCC 140-bus test systems against Volt/VAR and other baselines. In the IEEE 14-bus study, the controller reduces the mean data center disconnection rate by 44.8% and grid-demand loss by 27.4% versus the best baseline.

- **Technical:** Ride-through-aware voltage control assesses voltage deviations against facility-specific voltage-duration ride-through margins under limited reactive-power support.
- **Business:** Incorporating data center ride-through requirements into reactive-power allocation improves data center retention and reduces grid-demand loss during faults.
- **Technical:** In the IEEE 14-bus study, the proposed controller cuts mean data center disconnection rate by 44.8% and grid-demand loss by 27.4% versus the best-performing baseline.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08777>)

### [EMT Modeling and Coordinated Mode-Transition Ride-Through Control of Modern Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08614>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-05 · research*

The paper presents an electromagnetic transient model of a 100 MW data center with a UPS, battery storage, induction-motor cooling loads, a VFD-driven CRAH, and aggregated server load. A voltage-based state machine coordinates UPS modes, CRAH supply path, and ramped converter reconnection during grid disturbances. The model is benchmarked against the PSSE PERC1 aggregate model under a three-phase fault and LVRT/HVRT events, with close agreement in voltage and active-power trajectories.

- **Technical:** A voltage-based state machine coordinates UPS operating mode, CRAH supply path, and ramped grid-side converter reconnection after a disturbance clears.
- **Technical:** The EMT model resolves converter and motor-reacceleration transients that the phasor-domain PERC1 model cannot.
- **Business:** Large data centers respond to grid voltage disturbances in ways that can affect bulk-system stability.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08614>)

### [Joint Electricity Price and Load Forecasting for Data Center Battery Dispatch Using Polynomial Diffusion Models](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08470>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-05 · research*

The paper proposes a real-time battery energy storage dispatch framework for data centers that uses polynomial diffusion models to forecast electricity prices and data center load. The forecasts feed a dynamic programming approach aimed at minimizing electricity procurement costs. The authors report 2.63 times the cost savings of a rule-based strategy across the evaluated battery configurations.

- **Technical:** Polynomial diffusion models are used to forecast both electricity prices and data center loads, with closed-form conditional expectations feeding a dynamic programming dispatch framework.
- **Business:** The proposed battery dispatch approach achieves 2.63 times the cost savings of a rule-based strategy across evaluated configurations.
- **Business:** Battery storage shifts data center electricity purchases from high-price to low-price periods to reduce operating costs.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08470>)

### [Data Center Agglomeration and Power Infrastructure Capacity in the United States](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06737>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-04 · research*

This arXiv study builds a county-level US panel dataset from 2010 to 2024 that combines data center building footprints with power-plant additions. Using Double Machine Learning, it estimates that growth in data center floor area causes increases in nearby power-generation capacity, with lagged, event-study, and placebo checks. The authors report stronger effects in the West and negative displacement effects in resource-constrained Northeastern counties.

- **Technical:** Double Machine Learning with five-fold cross-fitting, conditioned on county and year fixed effects and socioeconomic covariates, is used to estimate causal effects.
- **Business:** Growth in data center floor area is associated with a significant increase in nearby power-generation capacity (ATE=1.982, CI \[1.25, 2.72\]).
- **Business:** The effect peaks at t=3, which the authors match to typical utility-scale grid-construction lead times, and effects are strongest in the West.
- **Business:** Negative displacement-related effects appear in resource-constrained Northeastern counties.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06737>)

### [Transmission Expansion with Large, Discrete Data Center Realization Uncertainty](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05958>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-04 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes a project-realization-based framework for transmission expansion planning under uncertain data center realization. It models candidate data center projects as alternative realization scenarios with associated probabilities and compares stochastic and deterministic planning approaches through numerical case studies.

- **Technical:** Candidate data center projects are modeled as alternative realization scenarios with associated probabilities within a common planning framework.
- **Technical:** Stochastic planning uses realization probabilities to hedge multiple plausible futures, versus deterministic approaches that either overbuild or prematurely screen out projects.
- **Business:** The stochastic approach is presented as offering a more balanced tradeoff between transmission investment and reliability exposure, under both correctly specified and misspecified probabilities.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05958>)

### [Adaptive Grid-Mode-Aware Hybrid Energy Storage Control for AI Data Center Oscillation Mitigation](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05939>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-04 · research*

An arXiv paper proposes a grid-mode-adaptive hybrid energy storage control framework to mitigate oscillatory power swings from AI data center demand. The method uses sliding-window N4SID identification to retune a supercapacitor's modal suppression branch when the grid mode shifts.

- **Technical:** Oscillatory components in AI data center demand can cause large frequency deviations when they coincide with vulnerable grid modes.
- **Technical:** A sliding-window N4SID estimator identifies the local grid natural frequency from grid-side power and frequency measurements to retune the supercapacitor suppression branch.
- **Technical:** In a grid-mode shift from 0.50 to 1.35 Hz, the adaptive method reduces post-change grid-side modal power from 8.78 MW to 2.02 MW, a 76.94% reduction, versus a fixed-target controller.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05939>)

### [Grid-Mode-Aware AI Data Center Power Smoothing via Bayesian-Optimized Hybrid Energy Storage System](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03436>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-02 · research*

An arXiv paper proposes a real-time control framework for a hybrid battery and supercapacitor energy storage system to smooth volatile AI data center power demand. Bayesian optimization is used offline to tune control parameters, and simulations report reduced 75-160 MW demand swings within a 10 MW band.

- **Technical:** A hybrid energy storage system combining a BESS and a supercapacitor uses frequency-decoupling and active modal damping to mitigate oscillatory AI data center load.
- **Technical:** Offline Bayesian Optimization tunes real-time control parameters, avoiding the computational burden of online receding-horizon optimization.
- **Business:** Simulations report that the approach keeps AI data center demand fluctuations largely within a 10 MW target band to support grid stability.

### [Dynamic Modeling of Transmission Networks, Grid-Following Inverters, and UPS-Based AI Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03279>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-02 · research*

The paper presents dynamic models of a transmission network with synchronous generators and grid-following inverters, along with average models of AI data centers. Each data center includes an IT load driven by a measured GPU power trace, an induction-motor cooling load, and a double-conversion UPS with battery and converters. The authors describe smooth surrogates for non-smooth operations and how each model is initialized at its power-flow operating point.

- **Technical:** AI data centers are modeled with an IT load driven by a measured GPU power trace, an induction-motor cooling load, and a constant-impedance load.
- **Technical:** A double-conversion UPS serving the IT load is modeled with rectifier, grid-forming output inverter, battery, and bidirectional DC-DC converter.
- **Technical:** Non-smooth operations are replaced with smooth surrogates so the discretized model stays continuously differentiable for the nonlinear solver.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03279>)

### [Multi-Timescale Deliverability of Shared Hybrid Storage for AI Data Center Campuses](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41249>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-30 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes a multi-timescale scheduling model for a shared hybrid energy storage system that mitigates power fluctuations at an AI data center campus. The model separates commanded from delivered power at the campus point of common coupling and is evaluated with the NLR GenAI Power Profiles Dataset, reporting reduced PCC and fast integral absolute error.

- **Technical:** A two-layer model passes five-minute scheduled power, energy state, and reserve capacity to a causal fast response model that distinguishes commanded from delivered power.
- **Technical:** Case studies show reductions in both PCC and fast integral absolute error while retaining most of the improvement available under ideal response.
- **Business:** Shared storage at the campus bus constrains the system to a single point of common coupling injection, relevant to how AI campuses manage grid-facing power variability.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41249>)

### [Market Efficiency and Capacity Investment under Flexible Price-Making Loads: A Computational Study](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41196>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-30 · research*

An arXiv study models a large flexible load such as a data center as a price-making consumer leading the system operator in a Stackelberg game. It compares the consumer's temporal and spatial load shifting with a cost-minimizing planner's over a simulated RTS-GMLC year. The authors report strategic shifting captures 75% (temporal) and 93% (spatial) of planner savings, and relate flexibility to thermal capacity, transmission, and renewables.

- **Technical:** Strategic temporal and spatial shifting capture 75% and 93% of the planner's savings, with the remainder mostly forgone by withholding flexibility to protect favorable prices.
- **Technical:** Scarce capacity and abundant renewables raise the share captured by temporal shifting, while scarce transmission lowers the share captured by spatial shifting.
- **Business:** Temporal and spatial flexibility act as substitutes for thermal and transmission capacity, respectively, and complement renewables.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41196>)

The arXiv paper introduces LLM-PowerTrace, an open dataset of time-resolved, per-GPU power traces sampled every 100 ms across NVIDIA platforms for LLM fine-tuning and inference, with workload and system metadata. - **Business:** Time-resolved AI compute power draw, including peaks and ramps, affects data center infrastructure and power grid planning.

### [Data Center Load Reductions: Attribution, Measurement Limits, and Estimation](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38216>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-28 · research*

This arXiv paper examines how to tell whether a sudden data center load reduction was caused by a workload change or a switch to backup supply, using point-of-interconnection measurements. It finds the two mechanisms can look identical in quasi-static models, and proposes a workload-data-based estimator when POI data are insufficient.

- **Technical:** Workload reduction and switching to backup supply can produce identical POI voltage and current in a quasi-static constant power model.
- **Technical:** During transients, separation of the two mechanisms depends on reduction size relative to measurement noise, clearing time, workload dynamics, and recorder bandwidth.
- **Business:** NERC calls on transmission owners to install disturbance recorders at data center facilities to support root cause analysis of load reductions.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38216>)

### [Machine learning-guided real-time AC optimal transmission switching](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38180>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-28 · research*

The paper presents a machine learning-guided method for real-time AC optimal transmission switching, using a fine-tuned surrogate model to guide search over transmission line topologies. Candidate switching decisions are verified with exact AC-OPF solvers within a 50-second budget. On a 2,000-bus synthetic Texas grid under a data center buildout scenario, the authors report a 31.97% generation cost reduction and a 94.34% reduction in gross nodal load payments.

- **Technical:** A fine-tuned pretrained AC-OPF model estimates generation-cost reduction from opening transmission lines to guide combinatorial topology search.
- **Technical:** Candidate topologies are verified with exact AC-OPF solvers, so the surrogate does not need to guarantee feasibility.
- **Business:** Under a realistic data center buildout scenario on a 2,000-bus synthetic Texas grid, the approach reports a 31.97% generation cost reduction and a 94.34% reduction in gross nodal load payments.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38180>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-25 · research*

This arXiv study presents a two-layer optimization framework (PSO outer layer, rolling-horizon dispatch inner layer) for sizing PV, wind, and battery storage co-located with hydroelectric plants to supply data center loads. Two case studies, The Dalles Dam in Oregon and Conowingo Dam in Maryland, compare outcomes based on land and renewable resource constraints.

- **Technical:** Hydro dispatchability is used to balance daily and seasonal variability from PV and wind, reducing the need for storage or grid imports.
- **Technical:** At The Dalles Dam, favorable wind and land enabled 111.2 MW PV and 117.9 MW wind with no BESS required.
- **Business:** At Conowingo Dam, land constraints limited renewables to 130 MW PV, leading to higher grid dependence.

### [Hyperscale Data Centers as Wide-Area Damping Actuators for Low-Inertia Power Systems](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33742>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-24 · research*

The paper evaluates hyperscale data center loads controlled via DVFS as wide-area damping actuators for low-inertia power systems. Tests on the Kundur two-area and IEEE 39-bus benchmarks compare DVFS and battery actuators and report damping improvements and saturation behavior under several disturbance types.

- **Technical:** Bus 10 ranks highest among non-generator buses for the 0.647 Hz inter-area mode on the Kundur two-area benchmark.
- **Technical:** A classical washout-plus-lead-lag controller raises damping from 3.43% to approximately 6%.
- **Business:** Data center loads controlled via DVFS could provide grid damping services, with a 2 MW envelope non-binding for several disturbance types but binding under generator-trip-equivalent stress.

### [How AI Load Swings Reduce Datacenter Power Conversion Efficiency](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33300>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-23 · research*

An arXiv paper quantifies how repeated GPU power swings between compute and communication phases reduce efficiency in cascaded rack power conversion. Using an energy-based, cycle-averaged method and measured GaN PFC and LLC converter curves on a 240 V AC to 400 V DC to 51 V DC path, it reports a 0.88% loss for a 1 Hz swinging load and 1.66% when point-of-load conversion to the chip rail is included. The authors conclude that efficiency rated at average load overstates real-world performance.

- **Technical:** For a 1 Hz, 50% duty-cycle load swinging between 5 kW and 510 W, conversion efficiency falls to 95.97% versus 96.82% at the equivalent constant average load, a 0.88% loss.
- **Technical:** Including 51/48V to 1V point-of-load conversion to the chip rail raises the loss to 1.66% (89.45% vs. 90.96%).
- **Business:** Efficiency rated at average load overstates real-world performance, and the penalty grows with each load-dependent conversion stage between the grid and the GPU.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33300>)

### [Data Center Oscillation Isolation and Grid Support: Fundamental Limits and Source-Selective Control](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26812>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-19 · research*

The paper derives fundamental limits and designs a source-selective storage controller to isolate oscillations caused by fluctuating data center computing demand while providing prescribed grid support. The controller is designed via convex frequency-domain optimization and is tested on a single-generator interconnection and a modified nine-bus network.

- **Technical:** A coupled closed-loop model shows a direct isolation-support conflict when only grid-import-power feedback is used.
- **Technical:** Additional electrical measurements can remove the pointwise conflict but leave a conditional cross-frequency sensitivity limit.
- **Technical:** The local residual-feedback storage controller outperforms equal-order optimized comparators in oscillation isolation on the test networks while meeting grid response requirements.

*arXiv · 2026-10-17 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes a two-stage method for dispatching vehicle-to-grid (V2G) chargers at an airport campus where EV parking, airport buildings, and an AI data center share one grid connection. In a case study, adding 400 V2G chargers reduces high peak demand by 4400 kW (9%) and raises operator profit compared with smart charging, while the best owner contract pays 70% of energy savings.

- **Technical:** V2G discharge from parked EVs lowers the campus peak demand that drives a commercial tariff charged on the highest power drawn during peak hours.
- **Technical:** Battery wear costs are included so EVs discharge only when worth the wear; doubling V2G ports to 800 lowers peak further but reduces profit.
- **Business:** V2G is reported to cut more peak demand per dollar of investment than a 5 MW/10 MWh stationary battery and needs no extra land, with payback within 10 years if bidirectional chargers cost under about $1800 more.

### [Data Center Load Accommodation under QoS and Checkpoint-Induced Frequency Security Constraints](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22912>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-16 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes an optimal data center load accommodation method that jointly considers workload quality of service, checkpoint-induced frequency security, and grid adequacy. The authors report that the method avoids a 40.3% overestimation of accommodable capacity compared with a grid-adequacy benchmark. They find QoS is the main limiting factor and that frequency-security constraints tighten as training workloads grow.

- **Technical:** Synchronized checkpointing of training workloads can cause large, rapid load reductions that threaten grid frequency stability.
- **Technical:** Case studies show QoS is the primary limiting factor on data center load accommodation, while checkpoint-induced frequency-security constraints become more restrictive as training workloads increase.
- **Business:** Grid-adequacy-only assessments can overestimate accommodable data center capacity by 40.3%, so interconnection planning should also account for QoS and frequency-security risks.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22912>)

### [DC-TORQ: Fast Assessment of Data Center-Induced Generator Torsional Oscillation Risk Quantification](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22572>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-15 · research*

The paper proposes an analytical framework for assessing how periodic power oscillations from large data centers can excite turbine-generator shaft torsional modes. It derives closed-form transfer functions from data center power perturbations to shaft torque and uses them to set frequency-dependent limits and a screening criterion for connection buses. The method is validated on the IEEE benchmark model and applied to the IEEE 39-bus system.

- **Technical:** Data center periodic power demand can introduce forced oscillations that excite generator shaft torsional modes when frequency components are near a turbine-generator torsional mode.
- **Technical:** Closed-form transfer functions map data center power perturbations to torque in each shaft section, avoiding repeated EMT simulations across frequencies and load locations.
- **Business:** The framework provides a quantitative screening criterion for choosing candidate data center connection buses.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22572>)

### [Identifiability-Guided Composite Load Modeling of AI Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19565>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-14 · research*

The paper proposes an identifiability-guided calibration framework for a composite load model of AI data centers with IT, cooling, and auxiliary branches, for use in power-system transient stability studies. It evaluates the approach on 300,000 MIT Supercloud GPU power samples and 12,600 IEEE 39-bus fault scenarios, reporting higher aggregate and transient accuracy than an LSTM baseline.

- **Technical:** A workload non-equivalence result shows that voltage-only recalibration cannot reproduce workload-dependent responses of AI data center loads.
- **Technical:** Branch-level supervision preserves relatively high aggregate accuracy but substantially degrades cooling-component recovery when removed.
- **Technical:** Under unseen deep voltage sags, the proposed model achieves a transient R² of 0.750 versus 0.033 for the LSTM baseline.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19565>)

### [A Critical Review of Grid Integration Challenges and Flexibility Opportunities for Large-Scale Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19374>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-14 · research*

This arXiv review examines how large-scale data centers integrate with electric grids, covering resource planning, large-load interconnection, distribution feeders, market operation, power quality and asset management. It compares load modeling approaches and assesses flexibility options such as UPS and battery control, workload flexibility, renewable co-location and clean firm supply. The central finding is that peak demand and annual energy matching alone cannot establish reliable or sustainable integration.

- **Technical:** Data centers combine high rack densities, converter-dominated interfaces, thermally coupled cooling and UPS systems, which distinguish them from conventional commercial loads.
- **Technical:** Distribution-level issues such as harmonic resonance, ride-through and reconnection, transformer derating, cable ampacity and neutral-conductor heating receive particular attention.
- **Business:** Credible grid assessment must consider hourly and locational supply, network deliverability, marginal emissions, backup obligations and verified operating capability, not only requested peak demand and annual energy matching.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19374>)

### [Impact of Security Modeling Fidelity on Hyperscale Data Center Hosting Capacity Assessment](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18405>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-13 · research*

This arXiv paper defines data center hosting capacity as the maximum time-invariant active power demand at a single bus over a 24-hour peak-day horizon. It compares four grid models of increasing fidelity on a modified IEEE 24-bus system. The authors report that the N-SCUC model yields an average of 444 MW, and omitting N-1 security raises this to 532 MW, a 19.8% overestimate.

- **Technical:** Omitting N-1 branch-contingency security raises the estimated bus-level data center hosting capacity from 444 MW to 532 MW, a 19.8% overestimation.
- **Technical:** Simpler models (DC-OPF and basic SCUC) further overestimate hosting capacity, indicating a trade-off between model complexity and estimation accuracy.
- **Business:** Grid-side modeling assumptions materially affect how much data center load a transmission bus can be assumed to host, which bears on interconnection planning.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18405>)

### [Impact of Data Center Load on Co-Optimized Generation and Transmission Expansion Planning with Embedded Unit Commitment](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18385>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-13 · research*

This arXiv paper models how a 500 MW data center load spread across four buses affects co-optimized transmission and generation expansion planning on a modified IEEE RTS-24 test system with ERCOT-calibrated renewable profiles. It reports a 61.6% rise in 15-year net present value of system cost and a 45.2% rise in average LMP, and finds the optimal plan shifts from a 230 kV transmission reinforcement to a new 400 MW combined-cycle gas turbine.

- **Technical:** A mixed-integer linear program with unit commitment embedded is used to co-optimize transmission and generation expansion over multiple years.
- **Technical:** Existing renewables are near full utilization, so added data center demand is served almost entirely by incremental thermal generation.
- **Business:** Integrating the 500 MW load raises 15-year NPV of total system cost by 1,794 million USD, with operating cost making up 84.2% of the total.
- **Business:** Strategically sited generation can substitute for network reinforcement when serving data center load.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18385>)

### [Service-Aware Hosting Capacity Assessment of Edge Data Centers with Workload Flexibility](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17645>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-13 · research*

The paper presents a hosting capacity assessment model for edge data centers with shiftable workload scheduling, accounting for fixed, service-driven, cooling, and shiftable demand. It embeds the resulting active and reactive loads in a distribution power flow model. On the IEEE 33-bus system, load shifting raises maximum bus-level hosting capacity by 4.46% for MEC data centers and 9.89% for AI data centers, with negligible gain for CDN cases.

- **Technical:** Fixed peak load representations can miss service-dependent demand, cooling-related demand, and delay-tolerant workloads when assessing hosting capacity.
- **Technical:** Workload execution is optimized under arrival causality, maximum delay, and daily energy balance constraints, then embedded in a distribution power flow model checking feeder current and voltage limits.
- **Business:** Shifting workloads raises maximum bus-level hosting capacity by 4.46% for MEC data centers and 9.89% for AI data centers on the IEEE 33-bus system, while CDN shows negligible gain under the assumed cache windows.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17645>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-12 · research*

The paper proposes a power-trajectory framework in which data centers declare predictable aggregate demand evolution as continuous trajectories before day-ahead market clearing. It analyzes settlement effects of representation-induced power imbalances and supply-side compensation, illustrated with a 5-bus case and a modified IEEE RTS-GMLC case.

- **Technical:** Interval-based day-ahead energy blocks do not retain predictable intra-interval data center demand evolution, which can mismatch ramp-feasible generation trajectories.
- **Technical:** Declared continuous demand trajectories are treated as exogenous inputs for constructing generation and price trajectories, with physical and economic temporal consistency established.
- **Business:** Trajectory-based settlement analysis quantifies the monetary magnitude of power imbalances and the supply-side compensation that may remain under temporally consistent clearing.

This arXiv paper presents a framework for integrating large hyperscale AI data centers into power systems, linking reliability and affordability to load forecast uncertainty and multi-scale temporal variation. It analyzes measured GPU power traces from a public generative-AI workload dataset, which show tonal spectra and workload-dependent variance bands. The authors argue these loads warrant spectral description and affect reserves, ramping, voltage stability and harmonic interactions.

- **Business:** AI data center loads affect reserve requirements, ramping products, voltage stability, ride-through performance and reliability and cost implications for grid planning.

### [Virtual Transmission Lines from Data Center Workload Migration: Coordinated Multi-Year Transmission Expansion Planning](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13032>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-09 · research*

This arXiv paper models geographic workload migration between data centers as a virtual transmission line that shifts nodal data-center demand without moving physical electricity. It proposes a mixed-integer linear programming model that co-optimizes multi-year transmission expansion and workload migration, evaluated on modified IEEE 14-bus and 118-bus systems.

- **Technical:** Coordinating virtual transmission lines from workload migration with physical transmission expansion reduces total system cost, unserved data-center load, and renewable curtailment.
- **Technical:** VTL-aware planning requires one fewer candidate physical line than build-only planning, but VTL alone cannot meet system demand.
- **Business:** VTL provides the greatest value when transfer capacity is tight, construction costs are high, load growth is rapid, and data-center flexibility is greater.

### [Trajectory Sensitivity-Based Dynamic Assessment of an AI Data Center Under Contingencies](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09393>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-07 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes a trajectory sensitivity-based framework for assessing the dynamic response of a self-contained AI data center supplied by a 2 kV DC microgrid. The method models data-hall load profiles, a VSC, BESS units, supercapacitors, and synchronous sources, and is validated against full nonlinear simulations with ±15% parameter perturbations. The authors report reducing evaluation time from seconds to milliseconds.

- **Technical:** Trajectory sensitivity is used to estimate system trajectories under simultaneous parameter perturbations, avoiding repeated full nonlinear time-domain solutions.
- **Technical:** The AI data center microgrid includes a voltage source converter, battery energy storage, supercapacitors, and synchronous-generation-side sources on a 2 kV DC microgrid with a grid-tied interface.
- **Business:** The reported reduction from seconds to milliseconds positions the approach as a scalable screening tool for AI data center dynamics.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09393>)

### [AI Data Centers Meet Electrical Grids: A Review of Power Challenges and Coordinated Solutions](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09060>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-06 · research*

This review examines how AI data centers affect power system stability, renewable integration, and low-carbon planning, tracing the power-delivery chain from accelerators and rack converters through UPS, storage, and microgrids to the transmission interconnection point. It covers mitigation strategies across device, rack, facility, and grid levels, and identifies research needs such as dynamic load models and carbon-aware scheduling.

- **Technical:** AI workloads create large power-electronic loads whose grid effects depend on location, temporal variability, and controllability, so annual electricity consumption alone is insufficient.
- **Technical:** Facility responses such as UPS and rack converters can amplify grid disturbances, while programmable workloads and controllable converters offer demand flexibility.
- **Business:** Lower energy per token does not necessarily reduce peak demand, electrical disturbances, or carbon emissions.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09060>)

### [Unlocking AI Data Center Interconnection Capacity Through Coordinated Grid and Data Center Flexibility](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05502>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-04 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes a day-ahead optimization framework that coordinates grid-side flexibility (conservation voltage reduction and network reconfiguration) with data center workload shifting and UPS storage to increase feasible AI data center IT capacity at a constrained interconnection bus. Simulations on a 116-bus model derived from the IEEE 123-node feeder report coordinated operation raising feasible IT capacity from about 10.5-10.6 MW to 15.3-16.0 MW.

- **Technical:** Grid-side flexibility, driven almost entirely by network reconfiguration, increases feasible IT capacity by about 42.5-45.0% at the constrained bus-60 connection point, while data center flexibility alone provides about 2.1-5.4% gains.
- **Technical:** Coordinated operation of grid and data center flexibility raises feasible IT capacity from about 10.5-10.6 MW to 15.3-16.0 MW, with the Inference result limited by the adopted UPS reserve requirement.
- **Business:** Coordinating flexibility on both sides of the interconnection can help AI data centers connect despite distribution network headroom limits that would otherwise delay interconnection.

### [How suboptimal is my stochastic network controller allowed to be? Completion certificates with application to power grids hosting AI data centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03275>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-02 · research*

The paper frames grid control for power systems hosting AI data centers as a stochastic control problem and bounds how far a deployed controller is from optimal. It builds lower bounds from path-integral control subsolutions and upper bounds from simulation. On the IEEE 118-bus system after a severe load loss, a simple generator controller is certified within 2.8% of optimal under homogeneous forcing, and repricing control authority reduces a 39% heterogeneous-forcing gap to 1.2%.

- **Technical:** Lower bounds come from HJB subsolutions built by completing the control geometry, and simulation gives the upper bound, so their gap certifies permissible suboptimality.
- **Technical:** On the IEEE 118-bus system after a severe load loss, a simple generator controller is certified within 2.8% of optimal under homogeneous forcing.
- **Business:** Under heterogeneous forcing, the gap is 39% with uniform control prices and 1.2% after repricing the same total authority, before any hardware is added.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03275>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-02 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes a long-term generation and transmission expansion planning model that adds a virtual transmission line built from a pair of battery storage systems, coordinated so they do not charge or discharge simultaneously. It is evaluated on a revised IEEE 24-bus system and reports a 12.3% reduction in weighted total congestion for a 0.3% increase in planning cost. The motivation mentions renewable growth and integrated data centers in deregulated power markets.

- **Technical:** Coordinating two BESS units so they do not charge or discharge simultaneously provides congestion relief without an explicit congestion cost in the objective.
- **Business:** Virtual transmission lines built from storage may offer congestion mitigation as a complement to standalone BESS at modest added planning cost.

PowerZooJax is a JAX-based benchmark suite for reinforcement learning in power system operation, offering five constrained Markov decision process tasks including a data center microgrid. *arXiv · 2026-09-23 · research*

This arXiv paper presents a cycle-space certificate framework for analyzing synchronization and transient stability of power grids under large, sudden data center load disconnections. It evaluates the approach on MATPOWER benchmarks and a stylized 2.7-GW 39-bus event.

- **Technical:** The exact strict-cohesion static test admits 16.2% more loading than the sufficient Dörfler–Chertkov–Bullo screen on the 118-bus case.
- **Technical:** In a stylized 2.7-GW 39-bus event, transient margin can change by about a factor of two depending on where balancing power is supplied, even when final operating points are statically feasible.
- **Business:** Routine transmission disturbances can cause several gigawatts of data center demand to disconnect or transfer to backup nearly simultaneously, motivating grid stability analysis.

### [Forced Oscillations in Power Systems Induced by Data Centers Hosting AI Workloads](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27698>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-23 · research*

This arXiv paper analyzes how power swings from large data centers running AI workloads can excite poorly damped modes and cause forced oscillations in power systems. It uses transfer-function analysis to identify critical grid locations and characterizes instability mechanisms and the interaction of two oscillating data centers.

- **Technical:** Power swings from large AI-workload data centers can excite poorly damped power system modes, leading to forced oscillations that may cause flicker, equipment disconnection, or blackouts.
- **Technical:** Instabilities are classified as saddle-node bifurcations of the forced periodic response and impasse-surface encounters, analyzed with Floquet multipliers and the minimum singular value of the algebraic Jacobian.
- **Business:** The findings can help system operators define regulations on maximum load fluctuations permitted for data center facilities at specific grid locations.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27698>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-22 · research*

The paper proposes a hierarchical, privacy-preserving three-phase scheme for coordinating power grid operation with AI data centers. It uses a certified inner approximation of the AI data center security region, workload allocation with checkpoint alerts, and a checkpoint-aware two-stage robust optimal power flow. Numerical studies use a modified IEEE 14-bus system and a modified NYISO system.

- **Technical:** AI data centers can adjust workloads through dynamic voltage and frequency scaling, and periodic checkpointing can cause abrupt power drops and rebounds that affect operating reserves and congestion risk.
- **Technical:** The scheme exchanges only compact interface information between grid and AI data center operators, aiming to preserve privacy and avoid frequent iterative communication.
- **Business:** Grid and AI data center operators are generally unwilling to share proprietary data and decision-making authority, which motivates the privacy-preserving design.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26365>)

### [Communication Delay Robust Control of BESS for AI Training Load Smoothing](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22542>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-18 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes a hybrid battery energy storage system control strategy that combines droop-based grid-forming control with load-current-based compensation to smooth rapid power fluctuations from AI training loads at the data center point of common coupling. It models communication delay between load measurements and the BESS controller, and adds a predictor-based compensation method to reduce delay-induced degradation. The authors report validation through electromagnetic transient simulations across different grid strengths and time-varying delays.

- **Technical:** AI training loads have rapid power fluctuations because power demand differs between computational and communication phases, creating challenging ramp rates at the data center point of common coupling.
- **Technical:** A predictor-based compensation method is added to the BESS control structure to mitigate degradation caused by communication delay.
- **Technical:** The hybrid control uses grid-forming droop control for long-term power exchange and load-following compensation for short-term AI workload fluctuations.
- **Business:** Battery energy storage in data centers is presented as a mitigation option for AI training load fluctuations.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22542>)

The paper proposes an improved droop control method for DC microgrids based on voltage-locked loop synchronization. It separates control into a virtual DC machine for transient response and a virtual current source for steady-state power sharing, and validates the approach in an experimental microgrid setup. The abstract names fast-varying loads in AI datacenters as a motivating application.

- **Technical:** The proposed scheme splits control between a virtual DC machine that handles transient damping and a virtual current source that handles slow steady-state power balance.
- **Technical:** The method was validated in an experimental microgrid setup, not only in simulation.

An arXiv paper presents a phase-decoupled, model-calibrated power controller for prefill/decode disaggregated LLM serving on an 8x B200 node. The authors report up to +20.4% tokens/J at +3.5% mean end-to-end latency on Qwen3-Coder-480B versus +8.6% at +5.2% for NVIDIA's Max-Q profile, and a 32.3% electricity saving in a three-day run.

### [Unifying Power Flow and Electromagnetic Transient Modeling](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08933>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-08 · research*

An arXiv paper introduces SALT, a steady-state grid analysis framework that uses full-physics electromagnetic transient (EMT) device models instead of simplified power flow models. The authors report EMT-level steady-state accuracy with a 1x10^6 speedup and find that power flow analyses miss contingency violations that SALT captures.

- **Technical:** Power flow simplified device models are inconsistent with EMT models, so steady-state solutions do not match the time-to-infinity EMT response.
- **Technical:** SALT builds algebraic frequency-domain representations of state-space power device models and reports a 1x10^6 speedup over EMT with steady-state accuracy.
- **Business:** Inverter-based resources and data centers reduce inertia and operating reserves, so power flow approximations risk missing grid security violations.

### [Beyond Scalar Flexibility: From Eligible AI Workloads to Dependable Load Relief](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05406>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-04 · research*

The paper reconstructs hourly power observations from a 185-day trace of 155,410 GPUs to derive a workload-based flexibility envelope for data center load relief. It finds that eligible curtailment is a small share of load and that firmness declines with event duration, while aggregating clusters and scheduler deferral add limited gains. The authors argue that duration, reliability, portfolio and realizability terms should replace fixed flexibility percentages in interconnection and demand-response contracts.

- **Technical:** Immediate eligible curtailment averages 3.55 MW, about 12.1% of workload power and 6.35% of median facility power.
- **Technical:** A mean-calibrated scalar overstates relief by 17%, 25%, and 47% across the one-hour, four-hour, and 24-hour cases.
- **Business:** Replacing fixed flexibility percentages with duration and reliability terms could be written into interconnection and demand-response contracts.
- **Technical:** Aggregating 13 clusters raises four-hour firmness from 0.38 to 0.66, but cross-cluster covariance limits the gain.

### [Grid-Mode-Aware Model Predictive Control of Hybrid Energy Storage Systems for AI Data Center Power Smoothing](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04398>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-03 · research*

The paper proposes a grid-mode-aware model predictive control framework that coordinates a battery and supercapacitor hybrid energy storage system to smooth highly variable AI data center power demand. The method suppresses grid-side power components tied to vulnerable oscillatory modes, and the authors report simulation results on effectiveness and computational efficiency.

- **Technical:** A fix-and-re-optimize algorithm is developed to solve each optimization problem efficiently for real-time use and to prevent simultaneous charging and discharging.
- **Business:** Mitigating load-induced grid oscillations is presented as a way to enable grid-friendly integration of highly variable AI data center loads.

### [Hosting Capacity Assessment of Data Centers with Voltage Ride-Through Capability in Power Systems](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03030>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-02 · research*

The paper presents a co-planning framework that couples transmission-level fault simulation with an internal data center ride-through model to assess data center hosting capacity under voltage disturbances. A case study on the IEEE 118-bus system finds that internal voltage ride-through capability can be a binding interconnection constraint, and that grid-forming operation gives greater ride-through margin than grid-following under the same faults.

- **Technical:** Internal voltage ride-through capability, including UPS converter headroom, can be a binding constraint on data center interconnection, and steady-state planning alone can overestimate feasible capacity.
- **Technical:** In the reduced-order models studied, grid-forming mode provides greater ride-through margin than current-limited grid-following mode under the same network faults.
- **Business:** VRT constraints can change both total data center hosting capacity and how that capacity is allocated across candidate interconnection buses.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03030>)

### [Flexible Training Workloads in Large-Scale AI Data Centers for Transient-Stability Support in Transmission-Constrained Power Systems](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30901>)

*arXiv · 2026-08-31 · research*

The paper proposes training-induced load surge (TILS), a demand-side strategy that restarts or increases flexible AI training workloads in data centers after fault clearing to support transient stability. It evaluates the approach in a single-machine infinite-bus system, the IEEE 39-bus system, and a large-scale Korean power system, reporting increased transient-stability-constrained generation limits. Results depend on siting, response size, activation timing, and available electrical headroom.

- **Technical:** Upward load response from AI training workloads, activated after fault clearing, can reduce accelerating-power imbalance and limit first-swing rotor-angle excursion.
- **Technical:** Siting data center load at buses with stronger electrical influence on critical generators gives larger generation-limit increases.
- **Business:** AI data center load flexibility could provide complementary transient-stability support to grid operators when electrical headroom and reliable grid-triggered activation exist.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30901>)

### [Minimizing Grid Interconnection Capacity Requirements for AI Data Centers: A Developer-Side Planning Framework with Onsite Resources and Workload Flexibility](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29359>)

*arXiv · 2026-08-29 · research*

The paper presents ICP-AI, a planning framework for AI data center developers that minimizes grid import capacity under an onsite investment budget by jointly sizing PV and battery storage and scheduling deadline-constrained workload flexibility. It reports capacity reductions of roughly 4.4% to 13.3% across budgets and load profiles, and checks temporal sensitivity by solving over a full 8,760-hour chronology.

- **Business:** Securing grid interconnection capacity can take longer than constructing the facility, delaying AI data center deployment for years.
- **Technical:** At a $10M budget, 5% flexible load with a 1 h deferral window reduces BESS capacity from 15.30 to 4.87 MWh while raising capacity reduction from 4.43% to 4.84%.
- **Technical:** Interconnection capacity reduction depends strongly on the planning environment, reaching 13.3% for a more diurnal load at a $100M budget.
- **Business:** The framework provides an investment-interconnection frontier to support capital allocation and early project planning in constrained grid environments.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29359>)

### [Mitigating Forced Oscillations in Power Systems via Data-Enabled Predictive Control](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26975>)

*arXiv · 2026-08-27 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes Data-Enabled Predictive Control (DeePC) to damp sustained forced oscillations in power systems, which the abstract says are driven by large cyclic loads such as data centers. The authors compare DeePC with a conventional power system stabilizer on a multi-machine two-area system and report superior damping that depends on dataset quality.

- **Technical:** Large cyclic loads such as data centers can drive sustained forced oscillations in power systems.
- **Technical:** DeePC builds control actions from measured trajectories without an explicit system model, unlike fixed-parameter power system stabilizers.
- **Technical:** DeePC effectiveness depends critically on the quality and representativeness of the historical data used in the Hankel matrix.

### [Loadability Limits Under Periodic Load Forcing](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21256>)

*arXiv · 2026-08-21 · research*

This arXiv letter studies loadability limits for power systems when part of the demand varies periodically, as with data-center loads. It shows the steady state becomes a forced periodic orbit whose loadability limit differs from the static limit and depends on forcing frequency.

- **Technical:** Under periodic load forcing, the generic instability is a cyclic fold of the orbit rather than a saddle-node of equilibria, so the limit depends on forcing frequency.

- **Business:** Static loadability screening used for interconnecting large new loads may misestimate limits for data-center-type periodic loads.

- **Technical:** Homogeneous server utilization overstates line-loading variability by 17%-46% relative to heterogeneous utilization, depending on intra-cluster workload correlation.

This arXiv paper presents DC-CLM, a workload-aware extension of the WECC composite load model that represents UPS-supported IT demand, motor and VFD cooling loads, and training, inference, and idle workload profiles. It is implemented in MATLAB/Simulink and evaluated under fault-induced delayed voltage recovery for a study system with a 100 MW conventional load and a 200 MW data-center load.
