### [Modeling Liquid-Cooled Data Centers in EnergyPlus](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33148>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-23 · research*

An arXiv paper presents a native EnergyPlus object that models direct-to-chip liquid cooling for chilled-water and warm-water architectures within whole-building simulations. The authors validate it against a Modelica implementation across 21 operating conditions and report a case study where warm-water liquid cooling lowers annual PUE from 1.43 to 1.25 compared with an air-cooled baseline.

- **Technical:** The EnergyPlus object couples facility-water loops, rack-side secondary loops, coolant distribution units, and chip-junction temperatures in a single solver.
- **Technical:** Validation reports loop-temperature RMSE at or below 0.021 K against a 0.1 K acceptance threshold, including two full-year runs at one-minute resolution.
- **Business:** The case study reports warm-water liquid cooling reduces annual PUE from 1.43 to 1.25 and cuts total facility energy by 37.9% versus air cooling.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33148>)

### [Job Class Thermal Intent Aware Liquid Cooling Allocation for AI Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30785>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-25 · research*

An arXiv paper proposes Job-Class Thermal Intent (JCTI), which passes job class information from the scheduler to the liquid cooling controller so coolant can be staged before heat rises. Reported Monte Carlo trials over 120 paired runs show 56.4% fewer thermal violations and 60.2% less cumulative overshoot compared with a PI control loop. The authors also argue the approach can reduce sudden grid demand swings for gigawatt-scale AI data centers.

- **Technical:** Cooling loops typically react only after a temperature sensor detects a rise, which can take 30 to 50 seconds; JCTI uses scheduler knowledge of incoming jobs to pre-stage coolant.
- **Technical:** Thermal signatures per job class were derived from MLPerf GPU power traces, and arrival patterns were tuned against Alibaba cluster data.
- **Business:** Reported results show 56.4% fewer thermal violations and 60.2% less cumulative overshoot than a straight PI loop across 120 paired Monte Carlo trials.
- **Business:** The authors suggest thermally aware scheduling could smooth power swings and improve load prediction on the grid side.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30785>)

### [Dissecting the Carbon-SLO Tension in Geo-Distributed LLM Serving](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14571>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-10 · research*

The paper presents a latency-centric geo-distributed LLM inference simulator calibrated with hardware latency and power measurements, to study carbon savings under service-level objectives. It formulates greedy, ILP-based, iterative ILP, and facility MILP schedulers, reporting that the MILP option cuts carbon emissions by up to 60% versus round-robin with an average 3% SLO violation.

- **Technical:** Modeling queueing and continuous batching in inference engines is needed to capture latency dynamics when studying carbon-aware scheduling under strict SLOs.
- **Technical:** A facility MILP scheduler that prices the energy of batched requests collectively gives the best carbon-SLO balance among the schedulers evaluated, with up to 60% carbon reduction versus round-robin and average 3% SLO violation.
- **Business:** Carbon savings grow with the spread of carbon intensity across datacenters and shrink as system load increases.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14571>)

### [Equinix data center spills gallons of diesel in Hackensack river](<https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/technology/tech-news/equinix-data-center-spills-thousands-of-gallons-of-diesel-in-hackensack-river-new-jersey-mayor-attributes-it-to-a-computer-glitch-department-claims-no-observed-impacts-on-local-wildlife/articleshow/134699081.cms>)

*timesofindia.indiatimes.com · 2026-10-05 · news*

Equinix's NY2 data center in Secaucus, New Jersey, spilled about 5,000 gallons of diesel from a storage tank into an unnamed tributary of the Hackensack River, according to NJDEP. Crews recovered about 3,500 gallons, and the department reported no observed wildlife impacts and no nearby drinking water intakes. Equinix attributed the release to two broken valves, while the mayor initially cited a computer glitch.

- **Technical:** Diesel fuel from backup generator storage tanks is the source of the spill; the article notes diesel is commonly used to power data center backup generators.
- **Business:** The spill is the latest of several diesel incidents at New Jersey data centers, with this one larger than the 2017 and 2022 cases.
- **Business:** Regulatory notification involved NJDEP and the Hudson Regional Health Commission, and cleanup was handled by Clean Harbors.

### [Aging-Aware Online Distributed Scheduling for Lifecycle Carbon Reduction in Geo-Distributed Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00605>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-30 · research*

An arXiv paper proposes a lifecycle carbon modeling framework for geo-distributed data centers that adds server aging and thermal-stress-driven replacement emissions to operational carbon. It presents an online, privacy-preserving distributed scheduling method using a Lyapunov framework and ZSP-ADMM, reporting up to 13.0% lower carbon emissions and 12.6% lower operational costs versus benchmarks.

- **Technical:** Workload-induced thermal stress accelerates nonlinear hardware degradation, which shortens server life and raises embodied carbon via more frequent replacements.
- **Technical:** A utilization-dependent exponential aging model is combined with workload scheduling to estimate long-term carbon costs from server degradation.
- **Technical:** The method uses an enhanced Lyapunov framework with time-varying queue shifting and a zero-sum perturbation-based ADMM to coordinate data centers online while protecting exchanged workload information.
- **Business:** Simulation results report up to 13.0% lower carbon emissions and 12.6% lower operational costs compared with benchmarks.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00605>)

### [Beyond the Last Truffula Tree: SustainAI - A Water-Aware, Closed-Loop Framework for Environmentally Accountable AI](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30747>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-25 · research*

The paper introduces SustainAI, a water-aware framework for AI deployment that combines real-time water metering, a hallucination-aware penalty model, and water-aware routing that considers regional water stress. In an evaluation with Small Language Models across geographically distributed data centers, per-inference water footprint varied about 11-fold, and a substantial share of water went to incorrect outputs.

- **Technical:** Per-inference water footprint varied roughly 11-fold across geographically distributed data centers, from 0.0477 mL to 0.5360 mL.
- **Technical:** A water-aware routing algorithm accounts for regional water stress when distributing inference workloads.
- **Business:** Across 1,335 runs, about 399 mL of water produced only 240 correct outputs, indicating resources spent on inaccurate responses.

### [Data center cooling choices shift water impacts across the grid: An integrated water-energy model for sustainable data center development](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25437>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-21 · research*

An arXiv paper presents an integrated water-energy model linking data center cooling and electricity demand to hourly economic dispatch and monthly subbasin water depletion. Applied to proposed developments in Michigan, it finds air-cooled facilities halve total water use versus evaporative cooling but raise indirect water consumption by one-third through higher electricity demand. The authors argue data center water and energy impacts must be assessed together.

- **Technical:** Air-cooled data centers halve total water consumption relative to evaporative cooling but increase electricity demand, raising indirect water consumption by one-third.
- **Technical:** Attributing indirect water use to generators and hydrologic regions, rather than the facility location, reveals depletion beyond data center sites.
- **Business:** Facility-level water reporting may overlook depletion impacts in regions affected by data center grid load, informing siting, design, and reporting practices.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25437>)

### [CATS: A Carbon-Aware Task Simulator for Reducing AI Data Center Emissions](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14775>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-13 · research*

This arXiv paper introduces CATS, a trace-driven simulator for carbon-aware scheduling of AI inference tasks across cloud data centers. It compiles a dataset of 140 cloud regions and carbon-intensity traces for 145 grid regions, and reports that spatial shifting cut CO2 by 38.4% versus a speed-first baseline while temporal shifting yielded 16% savings with 3.27% SLA violations.

- **Technical:** Spatial shifting of inference tasks reduced CO2 by 38.4% versus a speed-first baseline in a 24-hour, 600,000-task trace at fleet utilization of 0.37.
- **Technical:** Temporal shifting yielded 16% CO2 savings with SLA violations bounded at 3.27%.
- **Business:** About 50% of current cloud sites lie in medium-to-high carbon-intensity grids, indicating a siting-carbon mismatch.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14775>)

### [GreenPassport: Request-Level Carbon Accounting for Cross-Border AI Inference](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06784>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-06 · research*

The arXiv paper proposes GreenPassport Carbon Accounting (GPCA), a request-level method for estimating carbon emissions of cross-border AI inference across serving hardware, electricity mixes, and network routes. The authors report lower prediction error than EcoLogits in their public-data implementation and include a buyer scenario in a clean-electricity region showing a large per-request reduction.

- **Technical:** GPCA associates serving site, route, hardware, and provenance inputs with each request and estimates serving and route carbon per request.
- **Technical:** Against EcoLogits under the aligned accelerator-energy boundary, GPCA reduced median absolute percentage error by 56.3% and median absolute error by 15.5%.
- **Business:** In the buyer case, the clean-electricity CN-West scenario produced 0.0148 gCO2e per request versus 0.1220 gCO2e for the local service, an 88% reduction.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06784>)

### [InFactPlanner: Planning Sustainable Geo-Distributed LLM Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12915>)

*arXiv · 2026-08-13 · research*

InFactPlanner is a trace-driven framework for what-if analysis of sustainable AI data center deployment for LLM inference across single and geo-distributed sites. It estimates power, energy, carbon, water use, latency, and server utilization using query traces, hardware profiles, PUE/WUE parameters, renewable models, and grid carbon intensity. The authors report validation of their energy accounting within 10% of reference estimates and find that sustainability-optimal choices can differ from latency-optimal ones.

- **Technical:** The framework reproduces reference LLM inference energy estimates with less than 10% deviation.
- **Business:** Sustainability-optimal deployment choices can differ from latency-optimal ones.
- **Business:** The carbon value of a deployment depends strongly on the local grid mix.
- **Technical:** Models compare site selection, capacity placement, hardware, renewable integration, and carbon-aware routing before infrastructure is built.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12915>)

### [Oracle invokes force majeure on New Mexico AI data center](<https://qz.com/oracle-force-majeure-new-mexico-ai-data-center-092426>)

*qz.com · 2026-09-24 · investment*

Oracle sent a force majeure notice to the developer of its New Mexico AI data center, Project Jupiter, a unit of Blue Owl Capital, which would let Oracle delay payment if the campus is not online as expected in 2028. Oracle says the project remains on schedule, while Blue Owl says the notice does not change the project's financial commitments. The article also cites $18 billion in project debt, rising capital spending, and Oracle's negative free cash flow.

- **Business:** Oracle's force majeure notice would allow it to delay payment on the New Mexico campus if it is not online as expected in 2028.
- **Business:** Oracle's cash used for capital expenditures rose to $28.5 billion in the quarter ended August 31, up from $8.5 billion a year earlier.
- **Business:** The project carries $18 billion in debt that is reported to be trading at stressed levels.
- **Business:** Oracle reported negative free cash flow of $5.4 billion and plans to raise about $40 billion through debt and equity this fiscal year.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://qz.com/oracle-force-majeure-new-mexico-ai-data-center-092426>)

### [Winners and Losers If the Data Center Buildout Is Delayed](<https://www.apollo.com/wealth/insights-news/insights/daily-spark/winners-and-losers-if-the-data-center-buildout-is-delayed>)

*apollo.com · 2026-10-30 · investment*

Apollo's Daily Spark piece analyzes who would win and lose if the data center buildout is delayed, given compute demand exceeding supply. It argues owners of operating capacity, powered land, long-lead equipment makers and power producers would benefit, while hyperscaler earnings growth, developers, and AI-linked equities would be hurt.

- **Business:** Operating data centers and holders of powered land with grid access benefit from scarcity, since new competitors cannot arrive quickly.
- **Business:** A delay pushes developer cash flows out while interest and carrying costs continue, with leveraged or pre-lease-dependent developers facing refinancing stress.
- **Technical:** Lead times for transformers can reach up to 26 months, giving long-lead equipment makers pricing power.
- **Business:** The investment distinction is delay versus cancellation: a delay pushes cash flows out, while scarcity could unwind if supply catches up or demand softens.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://www.apollo.com/wealth/insights-news/insights/daily-spark/winners-and-losers-if-the-data-center-buildout-is-delayed>)

### [Capacity Markets for Large Loads under Supply-Chain Constraints](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06528>)

*arXiv · 2026-08-06 · research*

An arXiv economics paper models bring-your-own-capacity mandates and flexibility accreditation in capacity markets for large loads such as data centers facing shared supply-chain constraints. It compares welfare effects of the two instruments under different supply-chain stress levels and price caps, and notes spillover and penalty-design implications.

- **Business:** With efficient pricing, BYOC mandates mainly reallocate procurement between grid-built and self-built capacity and have little welfare effect.
- **Technical:** Under administrative price caps, BYOC mandates can improve static welfare by forcing data centers to internalize the full cost of capacity.
- **Business:** Applying current capacity non-performance penalties to flexible loads may give financial incentives too weak to induce truthful flexibility reporting.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06528>)

### [Cisco Catalyst C1300-24XTS Review A Neat 24-port 10GbE Switch](<https://www.servethehome.com/cisco-catalyst-c1300-24xts-review-a-neat-24-port-10gbe-switch-marvell/>)

*ServeTheHome · 2026-11-06 · engineering*

ServeTheHome reviews the Cisco Catalyst C1300-24XTS, a 1U switch with 24 independent 10GbE ports split between 12 10GBase-T ports and 12 SFP+ cages. The review covers the hardware layout, out-of-band management port, single AC power input, and internal design, and notes the switch was tested under load.

- **Technical:** The switch provides 24 independent 10GbE ports, with 12 10GBase-T copper ports and 12 SFP+ cages, and the copper and SFP+ ports are not combo jacks.
- **Technical:** A dedicated 1GE RJ45 port provides out-of-band management separate from the 24 data ports and the console connector.
- **Business:** The unit has a single AC power input, so A+B power redundancy requires an external automatic transfer switching PDU.

Sources: [ServeTheHome](<https://www.servethehome.com/cisco-catalyst-c1300-24xts-review-a-neat-24-port-10gbe-switch-marvell/>)

*GitHub · 2026-11-06 · engineering*

A GitHub repository providing a containerlab-based lab for learning F5 BIG-IP load balancing within a spine-leaf data center fabric. The lab uses SONiC EVPN/VXLAN switching, a Palo Alto firewall, two BIG-IPs, servers, and user PCs, all built by one script.

- **Technical:** The lab builds a spine-leaf fabric using SONiC with EVPN/VXLAN overlay for practicing BIG-IP LTM deployment.
- **Business:** Offers a hands-on, reproducible training environment for data center network and load balancer engineers.

Sources: [GitHub repository search](<https://github.com/aramidetosin/f5-bigip-sonic-evpn-lab>)

### [When Less is More: Emerging Network-Wide Use Cases for Hollow-Core Fibers in Optical Networks \[Invited\]](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08389>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-05 · research*

An invited arXiv paper overviews four network-level use cases for selectively deploying hollow-core fiber in hybrid SMF/HCF optical networks. It reports consolidated results across metro, national, and continental topologies, including reduced edge data center counts and lower network power per Tbps.

- **Technical:** Hollow-core fiber offers about 30% lower propagation latency than standard single-mode fiber, with much lower attenuation and nonlinearity.
- **Technical:** Selective HCF upgrades capture a significant share of the benefit of a full network overhaul, with use-case-specific HCF budgets.
- **Business:** Edge data center consolidation with HCF can reduce the number of edge data centers by up to 50% at 6-24% of fiber length.

### [Rondo: Controlling Reordering in High-Throughput Packet Spraying](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04594>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-03 · research*

This arXiv paper presents Rondo, a packet-level multipath transport for datacenter networks that controls packet reordering under packet spraying. It combines a multipath controller using ACK-based per-path capacity estimation with a packet scheduler that compensates for persistent path-delay differences. The authors report testbed and simulation results including up to 97.5% lower maximum out-of-order degree and up to 8.51x higher goodput with constrained receiver buffers.

- **Technical:** Packet reordering under packet spraying stems from transient inter-path queueing differences caused by control dynamics and persistent path-delay differences from path heterogeneity.
- **Technical:** Rondo pairs a multipath controller using ACK-based per-path capacity estimation and probing with a packet scheduler that adjusts transmission order to compensate for persistent delay differences.
- **Technical:** Reported results show up to 97.5% reduction in maximum out-of-order degree and up to 8.51x higher goodput with constrained receiver buffers.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04594>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-03 · research*

An arXiv paper presents Halley, a predictive control plane for optical networks in AI data centers, aimed at handling correlated failures of lasers, switches, and microrings. The authors report simulated goodput gains and reduced data center capacity needs under the proposed approach.

- **Technical:** Correlated failures of lasers sharing an enclosure, cooling, and power can cap pool availability at a fixed spare ratio regardless of pool size.
- **Technical:** Halley uses four components (LightReserve, FoldFit, ThermaLead, TriageGuard) with predictive control that defers to the deployed policy when uncertain.
- **Business:** Simulated effective goodput rises from 65.2% to 76.5% and per-megawatt training throughput is reported as up to 1.03 to 1.17 times baseline.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04408>)

### [Ordered Chaos: Towards Adaptive Multipath Load Balancing with Precise Fast Loss Detection](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41707>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-30 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes Ordered Chaos, an end-host multipath load balancing design for best-effort datacenter fabrics that splits flows into ordered, dynamically routed subflows. It adds Precise Fast Loss Detection, which uses probes to distinguish reordering from congestion, corruption, tail, and retransmission losses within one RTT. The authors report simulation, AI/HPC workload, and DPU prototype results, including up to 3x better tail flow completion time and up to 35% faster collective and training communication.

- **Technical:** Packet spraying balances load well but removes the ordering signals needed for fast, precise loss detection, while ECMP keeps loss semantics but uses path diversity coarsely.
- **Technical:** Reactive Subflow Spraying divides each flow into ordered, dynamically routed subflows, and Precise Fast Loss Detection uses probes to classify loss causes within one RTT.
- **Business:** Reported results include up to 3x improvement in tail flow completion time and up to 35% acceleration of collective and training communication for AI and HPC workloads.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41707>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-21 · research*

D-Pingmesh is a probing and fault-localization system for reconfigurable datacenter networks that co-designs pinglist generation with the topology and traffic engineering control plane. The paper reports fewer probing tasks than Pingmesh, faster pinglist generation than RD-Probe, and improved micro F1 in ns-3 simulation over the deTector PLL baseline, plus a testbed deployment.

- **Technical:** Control-plane-aware pinglist generation greedily admits probes with high interface coverage under the current TPE/TE snapshot.
- **Technical:** Incremental replay-and-repair updates pinglists across reconfigurations instead of recomputing from scratch.

- **Business:** At a 0.99 coverage target, the system uses 17.7x fewer tasks than Pingmesh on an 8-pod reconfigurable topology.

*arXiv · 2026-10-10 · research*

An arXiv paper proposes SG-MAT, a Stackelberg game with multi-agent transformer approach for resource re-optimization in multi-band optical networks affected by inter-channel stimulated Raman scattering. The authors report higher utilization gains and about 30% lower blocking ratio under moderate traffic loads than benchmarks, with comparable computational complexity. The work is motivated by connectivity between interconnected AI data centers.

- **Technical:** Multi-band transmission amplifies inter-channel stimulated Raman scattering, creating nonlinear coupling among services that complicates re-optimization.
- **Technical:** SG-MAT uses a leader-follower structure where a MAT-based follower generates channel reallocations and the leader selects critical service subsets.
- **Business:** Simulation results report about 30% reduction in blocking ratio under moderate traffic loads, versus benchmarks.

### [PCM: Programmable Congestion Management for Terabit RDMA NICs](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13130>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-09 · research*

The paper introduces PCM, a programming model that separates portable datacenter congestion control and load balancing logic from RDMA NIC platform integration. It reports implementing eleven congestion control and load balancing algorithms and deploying them across an Ultra Ethernet simulator, CPU-based UCCL, and on-NIC offloads on NVIDIA ConnectX-8 and Microsoft 1P.

- **Technical:** PCM lets developers write event-driven congestion management handlers that can be debugged in simulation and reused on real RDMA hardware.
- **Technical:** On ConnectX-8, PCM-Swift reaches processing capacity equivalent to one reaction per three 4 KiB packets at 800 Gbit/s while using one-fifth of the NIC's hardware threads.
- **Technical:** In UCCL, PCM matches native Swift's 1.56 Tbit/s goodput with 64 KiB chunk-level control.
- **Business:** Operators currently must port each congestion policy to every platform they deploy, which PCM aims to reduce by separating portable logic from vendor backends.

### [Parallel Architectures For Priority Scheduling In Programmable Data Plane Switches](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04587>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-03 · research*

The paper proposes two packet queuing architectures, P3PO and PPQA, for programmable data plane switches. Both split a large global priority queue into smaller parallel priority queues to scale accurate packet scheduling to speeds of 100 Gbps and beyond. The architectures are evaluated in the NetBench simulator using Web-search and Data-mining datacenter workloads.

- **Technical:** Accurate PIFO-based priority scheduling is limited at high network speeds by the requirement for line-rate packet sorting.
- **Technical:** P3PO uses cascading priority queues with priority-based bounds to insert packets into the earliest queue that maintains ordering.
- **Technical:** PPQA distributes packets across independent priority queues by occupancy, using a demultiplexer for balanced queuing and a multiplexer to select the highest-priority packet at dequeue.
- **Technical:** Approximate schedulers such as SP-PIFO, AIFO and RIFO introduce priority inversions that degrade latency, fairness and flow completion times.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04587>)

### [Sparse Distributed Fiber Optic Sensor Placement in Metropolitan and Datacenter Elastic Optical Networks](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03338>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-02 · research*

This arXiv paper studies how the number and placement of distributed fiber optical sensing devices in all-optical metropolitan and data center elastic optical networks can mitigate failures. It evaluates placement heuristics and prediction windows using blocking rate, affected circuits, and downtime, and reports that 5% to 25% link coverage can yield large gains.

- **Technical:** The Greedy Shortest Path Coverage heuristic substantially reduces the number of circuits affected by failures when DFOS devices cover only 5% of links.
- **Technical:** Prediction windows of 15 ms (metropolitan) and 7.5 ms (data center) are reported as sufficient for just-in-time restoration at 5% coverage.
- **Business:** 25% sensing coverage performs comparably to full coverage, suggesting significant cost reduction opportunities.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03338>)

An arXiv simulation study uses ASTRA-sim with an NS-3 backend to evaluate how GPU server topology, tensor and expert parallelism, collective algorithms, and congestion control affect completion time for MoE inference across 32 GPU ranks. ### [AI Data Center Basics: What Is Linear Pluggable Optics (LPO)?](<https://blog.semtech.fr/ai-data-center-basics-what-is-linear-pluggable-optics-lpo>)

*blog.semtech.fr · 2026-09-21 · engineering*

The article explains linear pluggable optics (LPO), an optical transceiver design that moves equalization from the module's DSP to the host switch SerDes. It compares LPO's power draw with retimed and RTLR modules and notes the emerging standards landscape, including OIF CEI 224 and the LPO MSA.

- **Technical:** LPO removes the DSP retimer from the module, leaving CTLE, driver, modulator, and TIA, with the host SerDes handling primary signal conditioning.
- **Business:** Retimed 200G modules consume roughly 23 to 25 W, while LPO modules target about 10 W, which the article says yields substantial savings at 512 and 1024 ports.
- **Technical:** LPO may not suit high-loss electrical channels or high-reflection environments, where RTLR or fully retimed designs may still be preferred.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://blog.semtech.fr/ai-data-center-basics-what-is-linear-pluggable-optics-lpo>)

### [RoutingBench: Can Agentic Routing Analysis Scale to Production Datacenter Networks?](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22848>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-19 · research*

This arXiv paper introduces RoutingBench, a benchmark for evaluating agentic AI approaches to routing-path analysis in hyperscale networks spanning many datacenters. It compares in-context learning, iterative reasoning, and agent skills, and reports 99.5% accuracy on networks of 50K routers.

- **Technical:** Routing-path analysis requires global reasoning about how a local device change affects all relevant paths, which is the scalability bottleneck for agentic NetOps.
- **Technical:** Agent skills curated with the principle 'explore more; digest less' reached 99.5% accuracy on networks of 50K routers, per the paper.
- **Business:** The paper reports that agentic analysis scales beyond traditional symbolic analysis, while limits remain for inter-datacenter networks and compound changes.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22848>)

### [Scalable Packet Tracking on FPGAs for Erasure-Coded RDMA over Lossy WANs](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21774>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-18 · research*

The paper presents COMET, a fully hardware-offloaded packet-arrival tracking design on an FPGA-based NIC for erasure-coded multi-path RDMA over lossy wide area networks. It reports line-rate operation at 400 Gbps and support for six times more concurrent connections than SoC-based designs. The authors connect the work to linking multiple datacenters into a single AI factory.

- **Technical:** COMET is a fully hardware-offloaded packet-arrival tracking design implemented on an FPGA-based NIC for multi-path RDMA over lossy WANs.
- **Technical:** COMET sustains line rate at 400 Gbps and decouples on-chip memory footprint from link bandwidth-delay product.
- **Technical:** The cache-based architecture supports six times more concurrent connections than state-of-the-art SoC-based designs.
- **Business:** Extending RDMA across WANs is presented as necessary for interconnecting multiple datacenters into a single AI factory.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21774>)

### [QPS-ToR: A Parallel Iterative Switching Algorithm for Reconfigurable Optical Datacenter Switching](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09400>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-08 · research*

The paper proposes QPS-ToR, a scheduling algorithm for reconfigurable optical data center networks that replaces NegotiaToR's single-iteration iSLIP engine with a sliding-window QPS scheduler. The authors report up to 36% higher throughput and 82% lower flow completion time versus NegotiaToR in flow-level simulations with 128 ToRs, and better results than RotorNet on the parallel network topology.

- **Technical:** NegotiaToR's single-iteration iSLIP scheduler limits throughput to around 60%, and it gives all source-destination pairs equal priority regardless of queue length.
- **Technical:** QPS-ToR reduces the scheduling cycle from three-step Request-Grant-Accept to two-step Request-Grant with a sliding window mechanism, reaching around 90% throughput in one low-complexity iteration.
- **Business:** Simulated results show up to 36% higher throughput and 82% lower flow completion time than NegotiaToR under realistic datacenter workloads.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09400>)

### [A Wavelength Borrowing Architecture for Optical Data Center Networks - Extended Version](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04874>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-04 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes a spine-leaf all-optical data center network architecture in which wavelengths unused or lightly loaded at one leaf can be borrowed by leaves with higher demand. A tunable borrowing degree B controls reconfigurability, and the paper reports performance near that of a fully reconfigurable design at well below maximum B. The fabric uses AWGs, AWGRs, colorless OXCs and combiners, and is suited to pod-level traffic that varies over tens of minutes or longer.

- **Technical:** A tunable borrowing degree B sets wavelength reconfigurability from none to full, and near-full performance is reached well below the maximum B.
- **Technical:** Two-hop detouring combined with wavelength borrowing controls wavelength load while limiting detoured traffic.
- **Business:** The architecture avoids the complexity of a fully reconfigurable optical solution, using mature data-center-grade components.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04874>)

The paper presents an implementation of the DualPI2 active queue management (AQM) algorithm for the ns-3 network simulator, mirroring the official Linux kernel architecture. The authors validate the model against a physical Linux testbed across 25 Bandwidth-Delay Product scenarios and state that it can be used to evaluate L4S performance across topologies from data centers to home and office networks.

- **Technical:** The ns-3 model adds credit-based Weighted Round Robin scheduling, step-marking, and overload protection to DualPI2, which the authors say were previously missing from simulation.

### [PSP: Low-Overhead Packet-Level Load Balancing for Stale-State and Bandwidth-Asymmetric Networks](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08425>)

*arXiv · 2026-08-09 · research*

The paper proposes probabilistic state-proportional (PSP) dispatching, a packet-level load balancing algorithm for multipath Clos networks in AI data centers. It uses a band-based discrete state representation to reduce hardware complexity and suppress herding caused by stale state information. Simulation results on a cycle-accurate simulator are reported as outperforming JSQ and Random and remaining competitive with Top-k.

- **Technical:** Traditional hash-based flow-level load balancing cannot sense link states, causing hash collisions, hotspot congestion and tail latency in Clos networks.
- **Technical:** PSP replaces global sorting with local probability mapping using a band-based discrete state representation, reducing hardware complexity.
- **Business:** The authors claim PSP offers a lower hardware cost than Top-k while staying competitive on performance.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08425>)

### [Real-Time Nonlinear Mixed-Integer Model Predictive Control With Horizon-Tail Relaxations](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09187>)

*arXiv · 2026-11-06 · research*

The paper proposes a decomposition-based model predictive control method for nonlinear systems with discrete decisions that splits mixed-integer computations before and after state measurement to cut feedback delay. It relaxes integer controls over the horizon tail and derives error bounds. In a data center cooling example, it reports a 94% reduction in mean feedback computation time versus a state-of-the-art MINLP method, with under 1% closed-loop cost difference.

- **Technical:** Solves a continuous relaxation at a predicted state before measurement arrives, then solves a mixed-integer QP once the measured state is available.
- **Technical:** Fixes or relaxes integer controls over the prediction horizon tail while keeping an integer-feasible first control input, with derived error bounds.
- **Business:** In a data center cooling example, mean feedback computation time drops 94% with a closed-loop cost difference under 1%.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09187>)

### [Context Is Not Free: Carbon-Aware LLM Routing for Multi-Turn LLM Web Services across Geo-Distributed Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35230>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-26 · research*

The paper presents CarbonRouter, an online framework that routes LLM conversation queries across models, GPU types, and geo-distributed data center regions to minimize carbon emissions. It adds a cache-aware carbon cost model that accounts for recomputing KV-cache history when a session moves, and reports a 16.1% average CO2 reduction relative to FairCarbon in trace-driven experiments.

- **Technical:** Moving a multi-turn conversation to another deployment requires recomputing its KV-cache history, whose carbon cost grows with conversation length and can outweigh routing savings.
- **Technical:** A lightweight gate keeps a session on its cached deployment when it would likely move back and rebuild history at the next turn.
- **Business:** Reported carbon reduction is 16.1% on average versus FairCarbon, rising to 23.1% for sessions longer than 30 turns, with predicted answer quality and estimated latency also improved.

### [Converter-Aware Coordination of Computing Flexibility in Behind-the-Meter Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22972>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-16 · research*

The paper proposes a converter-aware intraday scheduling method for behind-the-meter data centers that coordinates computing workloads with photovoltaic generation, energy storage, and grid purchases while respecting converter and network voltage limits. The authors model tasks with a mixed-integer formulation and a four-node electrical model using second-order cone relaxation. Case studies report that coordinated scheduling achieves negligible inference shortage and passes verification against the original AC model, whereas fixed training cannot support full inference service within voltage limits.

- **Technical:** Ignoring converter and network operating limits can cause converter overloads, protection trips, and interruptions to power and computing services.
- **Technical:** Minkowski-sum aggregation and Shapley-Folkman convexification yield a linear inference-pool representation without unit-level binary variables.
- **Business:** Coordinating workload flexibility with on-site PV, storage, and grid purchases reduces grid dependence in behind-the-meter data centers.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22972>)

### [Capturing Electrical Windows Through Reconfigurable Optical Networks: Transfer-aware Scheduling of Multi-Region AI Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13061>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-09 · research*

The paper proposes a transfer-aware scheduling framework for multi-region AI data centers that coordinates training placement, checkpoint transmission, and optical circuit switching to improve local renewable energy use and reduce grid purchase cost. In a five-AIDC benchmark, the method reduces real-time grid purchase cost by 12.50% and increases local renewable energy utilization by 2.724 percentage points relative to static optical operation. The authors report that optical circuit switching helps most when renewable-rich or low-price windows shift across sites and network bottlenecks delay checkpoint delivery.

- **Technical:** Transfer-aware scheduling accounts for checkpoint transfer delays that transfer-blind scheduling overlooks, which can otherwise overestimate migration benefits.
- **Technical:** The framework verifies checkpoint delivery under shared-link capacity constraints and revises infeasible schedules using updated delay estimates.
- **Business:** In a five-AIDC benchmark, the method reduces real-time grid purchase cost by 12.50% versus static optical operation.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13061>)

This arXiv paper proposes a day-ahead dispatch framework that schedules LLM pre-training workloads in renewable-dominant AI data centers using checkpoint-domain workloads and feasible 3D-parallel GPU modes. In a case study of a 2048-GPU AI data center running two LLaMA pretraining tasks, the authors report a 20.02% operating cost reduction and renewable curtailment falling from 1.077 to 0.014 MWh compared with fixed continuous training.

- **Technical:** Checkpoint-aware 3D-parallel dispatch is presented as the source of practical grid-interactive flexibility, since checkpoint records alone do not create flexibility.
- **Business:** Scheduling LLM pre-training around renewable availability is reported to cut operating cost by 20.02% and sharply reduce renewable curtailment in the studied 2048-GPU case.

### [OrigaMIG: MIG-Aware VM Placement with a Neighborhood-Restricted BILP and Live Migration](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06646>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-05 · research*

The paper models MIG-aware VM placement on GPU-equipped cloud data centers as a binary integer linear programming problem aimed at request acceptance, consolidation, and reduced migration. It proposes OrigaMIG, which uses a greedy placement rule and invokes a neighborhood-restricted solver with live migrations, and reports simulation results against default and state-of-the-art policies on 256 A100 and A30 GPUs.

- **Technical:** MIG placement rules cause fragmentation and suboptimal allocation, which OrigaMIG addresses by calling the BILP solver only when a request cannot be placed or GPUs become fragmented.
- **Technical:** The solver runs on a small neighborhood of physical machines while the rest of the data center stays fixed, with solutions executed as live migrations.
- **Business:** In simulation on 256 A100 and A30 GPUs in 128 PMs, the approach keeps fewer PMs active and uses the least energy per admitted GPU-hour at every load tested.

The paper proposes an LLM-driven multi-agent framework for container placement in data centers that balances power consumption against affinity preferences under resource and anti-affinity constraints. ### [Integrated Thermal and Power Management for Wave-Powered Subsea Data Centers via Nonlinear Model Predictive Control](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34109>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-28 · research*

This arXiv paper presents an integrated model and nonlinear model predictive control framework that coordinates thermal management, flexible workload scheduling, wave-power use, and battery operation in a wave-powered subsea data center. The framework is evaluated with closed-loop simulations using job-level workloads from the MIT Supercloud dataset, along with parametric, Monte Carlo, and distance-correlation analyses. The authors report that battery and wave-generation capacity mainly affect battery availability and flexible-workload queueing.

- **Technical:** A nonlinear model predictive controller jointly optimizes the flexible workload power budget and the cooling command under thermal, battery, and workload constraints.
- **Technical:** Thermal behavior is modeled with a three-node lumped model covering IT equipment, recirculating nitrogen, and the pressure hull, with surrounding seawater as the thermal boundary.
- **Business:** Excessive renewable generation capacity may increase energy curtailment, according to the parametric studies.
- **Technical:** Flexible-job delay is relatively insensitive to the investigated parameters, while terminal battery state-of-charge depends mainly on battery energy capacity and wave generation capacity.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34109>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-15 · research*

An arXiv paper proposes a hierarchical receding-horizon controller for modulating cloud datacenter power under time-varying grid and infrastructure caps. It combines slow discrete server transitions with fast continuous worker throttling and reports tests on instances with over 15,000 servers.

- **Technical:** A hierarchical controller separates slow server reconfiguration from fast worker throttling, with throttling solved as a convex recourse problem via dual decomposition.
- **Technical:** Experiments on instances with over 15,000 servers, 200,000 workers and 1,400 services report no power cap violations and lower service impact than fast-only or slow-only baselines.

### [ETCInfer: An Energy-efficient Thermal-aware Cooling-joint Scheduler for LLM Inference in AI Datacenters](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15230>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-14 · research*

The paper presents ETCInfer, a scheduler for LLM inference in AI data centers that jointly selects CRAC cooling setpoints and adjusts per-GPU frequency and micro-batch size to reduce energy while meeting thermal and latency constraints. It reports up to 33.1% lower job energy, up to 92.9% lower thermal throttle exposure, and SLO violations below 0.7% at ambient temperatures up to 48°C.

- **Technical:** Joint control of CRAC setpoint, GPU frequency, and micro-batch size is formulated as a partially observable Markov decision process.
- **Technical:** Physics-informed models calibrated from telemetry estimate hidden thermal states and time-to-throttle before actions are applied.
- **Business:** Reported results include up to 33.1% reduction in total job energy and up to 92.9% reduction in thermal throttle exposure.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15230>)

### [Spatial LLM Workload Shifting Needs Foresight: Model Commitment for AI Data Center Operation under Power Grid Constraints](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09787>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-09 · research*

The paper proposes model commitment (MC), a mixed-integer linear programming framework that jointly schedules LLM model deployment and cross-site request routing for AI data centers facing power supply constraints. It models intertemporal coupling from model replica loading and maps prefill and decode latency requirements to per-replica served demand. Case studies on real-world data report a 100% service rate and a 29.0% reduction in total operating cost.

- **Technical:** Existing workload-shifting methods assume any data center with spare compute can immediately serve shifted requests, which can cause infeasible transfers and unserved demand.
- **Technical:** MC models the intertemporal coupling introduced by model replica loading and converts prefill and decode latency requirements into per-replica serviceable demand.
- **Business:** Case studies report a 100% service rate under time-varying grid conditions and a 29.0% reduction in total operating cost.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09787>)

### [Real-Time Control of Sustainable Data Centers: A Two-Layer Model Predictive Control Framework with Workload Flexibility and Heat Recovery](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16432>)

*arXiv · 2026-08-17 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes a two-layer model predictive control framework for real-time operation of data centers with on-site photovoltaics, battery storage, waste heat recovery, and district heating. The upper layer uses scenario-based stochastic optimization for intraday market participation, workload scheduling, and energy management, while the lower layer uses adaptive tube-based MPC to track dispatch references. Simulation studies reportedly show reduced dispatch deviations and imbalance costs compared with single-layer control.

- **Technical:** A two-layer design pairs stochastic upper-layer dispatch planning with an adaptive tube-based MPC lower layer that compensates short-term disturbances.
- **Technical:** Multi-horizon forecasting supports real-time decisions, and simulations cover clear-sky and overcast photovoltaic conditions.
- **Business:** Compared with single-layer control, the adaptive lower-layer controller reportedly substantially reduces real-time dispatch deviations and associated imbalance costs.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16432>)

The paper presents ClusterBench, a framework for cluster-wide continuous benchmarking that dispatches identical benchmarks to every node and repeats them over the cluster's lifetime to detect regressions. It reports on NHR@FAU clusters that performance variation within a component stays within 1%, while variation across nodes reaches 5%, and that the relation between performance and power, frequency, and temperature differs between air- and liquid-cooled nodes.

- **Technical:** Benchmarks are dispatched to every node in a single cluster-aware submission to validate an entire installation at acceptance and at regular intervals.
- **Technical:** Repeated measurements are compared against earlier runs to detect performance regressions from software changes such as kernel updates or new library versions.
- **Technical:** Cross-specimen performance variation reaches 5% on nodes identical by specification, while variation within a single component stays within 1%.
- **Technical:** The performance-to-power, frequency, and temperature relationship differs between air-cooled and liquid-cooled nodes.

### [Nvidia Study: AI Agents Rarely Need Long Context Windows](<https://i10x.ai/news/nvidia-long-context-compaction-agents-study>)

*i10x.ai · 2026-11-04 · research*

An NVIDIA preprint reports that frontier agents such as Claude Opus 4.5 and GPT 5.6 Sol keep most of their accuracy when their context is compacted into short windows across 13 benchmarks. The authors also measure that serving a 256K window costs 3.6x to 7x more per output token than a 16K window on open-weight models, arguing short contexts could cut memory and GPU needs.

- **Technical:** Compacting agent context to short windows (16K to 128K limits) changed accuracy by only a few points on most long-horizon benchmarks for Claude Opus 4.5 and GPT 5.6 Sol.
- **Technical:** On vLLM with H100 GPUs, a 256K context window costs 3.6x to 7x more per output token than a 16K window across tested open-weight models.
- **Business:** Retail API prices are flat per million tokens, so late tokens in long contexts are cheaper for providers to serve than they are to generate at high context length, which the authors say could reduce serving costs.
- **Technical:** Open-weight models were more fragile under tight compaction, with Qwen 3 Coder Next dropping from 53.0% to 3.6% on SWE-bench Verified at the tightest setting.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://i10x.ai/news/nvidia-long-context-compaction-agents-study>)

### [Energy-Aware Agentic Orchestration for Multi-Site Service Deployment](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14793>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-10 · research*

The paper presents an energy-aware agentic orchestration framework that treats LLM reasoning as a measurable part of the control loop for multi-site service deployment. It compares single-shot tool calling, ReAct, and plan-and-execute strategies on a Kubernetes testbed across two European data centers with per-pod energy attribution. It reports that reasoning strategy and intent complexity affect GPU energy during orchestration decisions, and that prefix caching reduced mean ReAct GPU energy by 29.4%.

- **Technical:** Prefix caching reduced mean ReAct GPU energy consumption by 29.4%.
- **Technical:** ReAct and plan-and-execute produced executable tool calls across all evaluated intents but required multiple LLM inferences, while single-shot tool calling was less costly but non-actionable.
- **Business:** For the evaluated lightweight workload, infrastructure-energy changes within the one-second window were insufficient to offset the measured GPU decision energy, so reasoning overhead matters when assessing AI-native orchestration sustainability.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14793>)

The article frames the launch against the AI pacing debate and Jensen Huang's stance on it, and notes Sentry has no shipping date.

### [ExaServe: Large-Scale LLM Serving on Exascale HPC Systems](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10812>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-09 · research*

The paper presents ExaServe, a pip-installable framework that turns a YAML specification into a reproducible large-scale LLM serving deployment. The authors deploy it on ALCF Aurora from 1 to 256 nodes, reporting near-linear scaling for non-streaming inference and a plateau for token streaming through a centralized proxy.

- **Technical:** Non-streaming inference scales nearly linearly to 256 nodes, reaching 27.1k requests/s (3.8M tokens/s) across 3072 vLLM replicas.
- **Technical:** A centralized proxy plateaus at about 4.7k requests/s for token streaming, even though model servers remain within the service-level objective.
- **Technical:** An O(N^2) Ray Serve control-plane bottleneck raises cluster bring-up to roughly 30 minutes at 256 nodes.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10812>)

### [Nvidia's AVO harness boosts Opus 5 to 100% on ARC-AGI 3](<https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-avo-reaches-100-on-arc-agi-3-demonstrating-a-frontier-level-general-purpose-architecture-for-long-horizon-autonomous-agents/>)

*developer.nvidia.com · 2026-09-04 · research*

NVIDIA describes AVO, a general-purpose autonomous coding agent architecture with persistent memory and a supervisor for long-horizon work. The architecture was first applied to GPU-kernel optimization, where a seven-day run produced attention kernels that outperformed cuDNN and FlashAttention-4 on DGX B200 systems, and it was then adapted to the ARC-AGI-3 benchmark.

- **Technical:** In the GPU-kernel study, AVO ran continuously for seven days, explored more than 500 optimization directions, and produced 40 committed kernel versions.
- **Technical:** On NVIDIA DGX B200 systems, the evolved multihead attention kernels outperformed cuDNN by up to 3.5% and FlashAttention-4 by up to 10.5% across evaluated configurations.
- **Technical:** Persistent memory carries forward prior implementations, evaluation results, and compiler and profiler outputs so the agent can resume rather than rebuild its search.
- **Business:** The post argues that performance, reliability, and security must be designed across the full agent system, not treated as properties of the model alone.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/nvidia-avo-reaches-100-on-arc-agi-3-demonstrating-a-frontier-level-general-purpose-architecture-for-long-horizon-autonomous-agents/>)

### [Cross-vendor byte-identical inference for a 72B LLM (AMD MI300X vs. Nvidia H100)](<https://zenodo.org/records/19882078>)

*zenodo.org · 2026-08-25 · research*

A self-published paper describes a protocol that produces byte-identical outputs from 72B-parameter LLM inference on NVIDIA H100 and AMD MI300X GPUs, binding each output to a signed, offline-verifiable receipt. It also describes a probabilistic spot-check verifier and reports benchmark and verification-cost results.

- **Technical:** Output hashes match byte-for-byte across fresh process launches in every measured configuration, and single-GPU bf16 eager-attention outputs are byte-identical between AMD and NVIDIA.
- **Technical:** Two-GPU tensor-parallel outputs differ between NVIDIA and AMD, which the authors attribute to NCCL-ring versus RCCL-fabric all-reduce topology.
- **Technical:** Receipts use a canonical CBOR schema with Ed25519 signatures, implemented in Go, Python, and Rust, and verification takes about 80 microseconds per receipt on a single core.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://zenodo.org/records/19882078>)

### [Nvidia Sentry: Secure AI Agents at Runtime](<https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/inside-nvidia-sentry-secure-ai-agents-at-runtime/>)

*developer.nvidia.com · 2026-10-23 · engineering*

NVIDIA describes Sentry, a security framework that monitors AI agents and enforces policies on their access to models, tools, and data from outside the host. It runs on NVIDIA BlueField-4 DPUs as part of the OpenShell reference architecture and uses DOCA Flow and DOCA Tower to firewall and inspect agent traffic at layers 4 and 7.

- **Technical:** NVIDIA Sentry runs on BlueField-4 DPUs in a separate hardware trust domain, so agents and host software cannot alter or disable its monitoring and enforcement.
- **Technical:** DOCA Flow implements a per-agent layer-4 firewall in silicon, and forwards permitted traffic to DOCA Tower, an Envoy-based transparent proxy that performs layer-7 inspection and TLS termination.
- **Technical:** Agent network traffic reaches BlueField-4 through a direct network I/O path that bypasses host OS and hypervisor packet processing.
- **Business:** The framework targets enterprises deploying autonomous AI agents that need enforceable access controls on models, tools, and data.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/inside-nvidia-sentry-secure-ai-agents-at-runtime/>)

### [Nvidia AIStore CVSS 10.0: Container Escape and Host Takeover](<https://mauri0x00.pages.dev/posts/shadowforge-aistore/>)

*mauri0x00.pages.dev · 2026-10-15 · research*

A security write-up describes CVE-2026-65162 in NVIDIA AIStore, an object storage system with an ETL engine that runs user-supplied Kubernetes pods on storage nodes. The author says the ETL API forwarded user PodSpecs without stripping security context settings, allowing privileged pods and host escape, and that NVIDIA's v5.0 fix removed the InitSpecMsg feature.

- **Technical:** The AIStore ETL API deserialized user-supplied Kubernetes PodSpecs and forwarded them to the Kubernetes API without inspecting SecurityContext attributes.
- **Technical:** Validate() checked operational constraints such as readiness probes and ports but not security settings like privileged and hostPID.
- **Business:** NVIDIA's fix in AIStore v5.0 removed the InitSpecMsg feature entirely rather than adding allowlists.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://mauri0x00.pages.dev/posts/shadowforge-aistore/>)

### [The Global Data Center Boom Is a Gift to Spies](<https://www.lawfaremedia.org/article/the-global-data-center-boom-is-a-gift-to-spies>)

*lawfaremedia.org · 2026-09-10 · news*

An opinion-style analysis argues that colocation data centers, where multiple companies lease racks in shared facilities, create physical and digital espionage risks, such as tenants or contractors tampering with hardware near rivals. It notes that Chinese tech firms are investing heavily in Southeast Asian data centers and that U.S. rules on cloud access and model-weight storage have been proposed or enacted.

- **Technical:** In colocation facilities, servers are swapped regularly by short-term contractors, which could let a tenant or technician bypass keycard doors, cameras, and access logs to plant surveillance gear or replace components.
- **Technical:** Shared infrastructure such as virtualization software, GPUs, and management and cooling systems carries vulnerabilities that can let one tenant access another's environment.
- **Business:** Data center vacancies are near zero for the third year in a row, so tenants take whatever space is available, and landlords rarely disclose who else occupies the floor.
- **Business:** Alibaba, ByteDance, and Tencent are investing billions in new data centers across Southeast Asia, where American firms are also seeking capacity.
- **Business:** The Commerce Department proposed know-your-customer rules in 2024 for U.S. cloud providers, and a January 2025 rule restricted where model weights can be stored.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://www.lawfaremedia.org/article/the-global-data-center-boom-is-a-gift-to-spies>)

### [90 days of attacks on AI infrastructure](<https://www.wiz.io/blog/ai-infrastructure-honeypot>)

*wiz.io · 2026-08-30 · research*

Wiz Threat Research reports 90 days of honeypot telemetry showing active attacks on AI infrastructure, including LiteLLM, MCP servers and AI agent frameworks. The findings cover remote code execution through exposed MCP servers, blind prompt injection against agent frameworks, and AI-specific post-exploitation tooling.

- **Technical:** LiteLLM MCP Gateway authentication flaw (CVE-2026-59822) returns an unrestricted object when token validation fails, so any Bearer token grants full MCP access.
- **Technical:** Command injection in LiteLLM MCP server test endpoints (CVE-2026-42271) lets attackers run arbitrary commands via the unvalidated command field.
- **Business:** A LiteLLM proxy often holds credentials for multiple model providers and may carry cloud IAM permissions, so one compromise can expose downstream services.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://www.wiz.io/blog/ai-infrastructure-honeypot>)

### [New evidence on data center employment effects](<https://www.brookings.edu/articles/new-evidence-on-data-center-employment-effects/>)

*brookings.edu · 2026-10-02 · research*

A Brookings brief summarizes the paper 'Data Centers and Local Labor Markets' by Dany Bahar and Greg Wright, which compares U.S. counties with built data centers to counties with announced-but-canceled projects. The brief reports that counties receiving a first large data center see data processing and telecommunications employment rise, with wages unchanged and modest home price increases. The piece also covers the policy debate over data center moratoriums, tax incentives, and electricity costs.

- **Business:** Labor markets receiving their first large data center see data processing employment rise 56% over the first decade of operations, and telecommunications rise 43%.
- **Business:** Wages were unchanged and home prices rose 2%-5%, according to the brief.
- **Business:** Virginia's data-center sales-tax exemption cost an estimated $1.6 billion in fiscal year 2025, which the brief notes may subsidize investments that would have happened anyway.
- **Technical:** The study uses about 1,500 U.S. data center facilities, 52 announced-but-canceled projects, and county-level BLS data from 2003-2024.

Sources: [Hacker News (Algolia API)](<https://www.brookings.edu/articles/new-evidence-on-data-center-employment-effects/>)

An Coimisiún Pleanála overturned Mayo County Council's planning permission for a proposed data centre in Killala, Co Mayo, developed by Mayo Data Hub Ltd, part of AVAIO Digital. The regulator refused permission on the grounds that the applicant had not shown a fixed grid connection, sufficient grid capacity, on-site renewable supply, or conformity with the Climate Action Plan 2025.

- **Business:** The permission was refused after an appeal by a local objector, reversing the council's April 2025 grant.
- **Technical:** The applicant failed to demonstrate a fixed grid connection agreement and sufficient grid capacity for the development.

- **Business:** The ruling states the development would likely have significant greenhouse gas emissions from fossil-fuel-based energy.

It frames AI data center load growth as an additional load-growth factor driving new capacity investment across U.S. ISO/RTO regions. The authors compare scenarios on cost-effectiveness and carbon emission reduction.

### [Clockwork: Scheduling Compound AI Systems in Space Data Centers](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00909>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-31 · research*

The paper presents Clockwork, a framework for scheduling Compound AI workflows on LEO-based space data centers under end-to-end latency SLOs. It has two components, Castor for selecting active orbital planes and component placements per constellation snapshot, and Pollux for building a low-cost schedule across predicted orbital evolution. The authors report serving 81% of instances at a 300 ms SLO and a 56% mean reduction in scheduling cost versus heuristic baselines.

- **Technical:** Provisioning for space data centers must be planned over time because orbital motion changes which satellites can serve an area of interest, and placement changes or plane activations carry costs.
- **Technical:** Castor jointly selects active orbital planes and component placements for each constellation snapshot, while Pollux links candidate states across predicted orbital evolution.
- **Business:** On the experimental two-shell constellation, Pollux reduces mean total scheduling cost by 56% on average and up to 63% compared to heuristic baselines.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00909>)

### [Heterogeneous Space Data Centers: A Key Paradigm for Future Non-Terrestrial and In-Orbit Services](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32260>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-23 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes heterogeneous space-based data centers made of large and small orbital nodes that provide AI computing and communication services. It analyzes solar power availability, visibility windows, latency-aware coverage, and energy requirements for low-Earth orbit configurations.

- **Technical:** The paper analyzes solar power availability, visibility windows, and latency-aware coverage for dawn-dusk LEO configurations.
- **Technical:** Heterogeneous space data center nodes are differentiated by computing capability, size, and power budget, with space-to-space and space-to-ground interconnection topologies.
- **Business:** The paper provides design guidance on trade-offs among node size, orbit selection, and service portfolio for AI infrastructure in space.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32260>)

### [Low-Latency MoE Inference in Space Data Centers: A Wireless-Power-Transfer Perspective](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30923>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-22 · research*

An arXiv paper proposes SpaceWPT, a framework that uses inter-satellite laser wireless power transfer to redistribute solar power among satellites hosting mixture-of-experts LLM inference in space data centers. The authors derive a two-sided water-filling strategy to balance power between donor and receiver satellites under transfer losses. Simulations with Mixtral-8x7B and Phi-3.5-MoE report up to 29.25% lower inference latency than without power transfer.

- **Technical:** Skewed expert activation in MoE inference creates uneven power demand across satellites despite uniform solar supply in a dawn-dusk SSO.
- **Technical:** A two-sided water-filling strategy decides whether each active expert donates, retains, or receives power based on water level and WPT link efficiency.
- **Technical:** Simulations report up to 29.25% lower inference latency versus no WPT, with larger gains for more compact formations and higher transfer efficiencies.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30923>)

### [BIER-DC: Stateless Multicast for In-Orbit Space Data Centers in LEO Constellations](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19462>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-14 · research*

This arXiv paper evaluates how satellite cluster sizes affect visibility and gateway outage for space data centers in LEO constellations. It proposes BIER-DC, a stateless one-to-many multicast protocol that separates inter-cluster forwarding from intra-cluster delivery.

- **Technical:** BIER-DC encodes compact cluster-level forwarding information in packet headers to reduce header length and byte-hop overhead.
- **Technical:** The protocol is compared against BIER, BIER-TE, and Yeti, with reported improvements in effective goodput and scalability.
- **Business:** Terrestrial cloud regions may give satellite data high delivery latency due to intermittent satellite-to-ground visibility.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19462>)

### [Space AI Infrastructure: Where the Edge Meets the Orbital Cloud](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16395>)

*arXiv · 2026-10-12 · research*

This arXiv paper proposes a roadmap for Space AI infrastructure, progressing from onboard AI to Space Edge AI and Space Cloud AI. It describes an architecture in which LEO constellations provide edge computing and backhaul, and formation-flying satellites in dawn-dusk sun-synchronous orbits act as space data centers. The authors report a case study claiming support for data-center-scale foundation-model inference for millions of concurrent users.

- **Technical:** The architecture pairs communication-centric LEO constellations for user-proximate computing and access/backhaul with computing-centric formation-flying satellites organized as space data centers.
- **Technical:** Space data centers are proposed in dawn-dusk sun-synchronous orbits, leveraging abundant solar energy in orbit.
- **Business:** The paper argues orbital AI services must be designed to be competitive with terrestrial data center services.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16395>)

### [Toki: Profiling HBM Performance on FPGA Systems with RISC-V Soft Cores and PCIe Host DMA Traffic](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26551>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-22 · research*

Toki is an open-source hardware-software framework for profiling HBM performance on FPGA accelerator cards, combining workloads on RISC-V soft cores with memory traffic injected from the host over PCIe DMA. The paper reports experiments on an AMD Alveo U55C card with up to 60 RISC-V cores and HBM2 memory, using real applications and microbenchmarks.

- **Technical:** Profiling jointly considers RISC-V soft-core workload execution and host memory traffic injected via PCIe DMA, which synthetic traffic generators alone cannot capture.
- **Technical:** Experiments on an AMD Alveo U55C card with up to 60 RISC-V cores measure the effects of core organization, memory access patterns, data locality, and memory controller contention on HBM performance.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26551>)

### [Bio-inspired Learning and Decision-Making with Probabilistic In-Memory Computing Hardware: Part 2](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11288>)

*arXiv · 2026-09-10 · research*

This arXiv report extends earlier work on an energy-based model for learning and decision-making under uncertainty, which uses stochastic Langevin dynamics. It argues that GPU execution with high-bandwidth memory (HBM) limits the scalability of such models, and uses a transaction-level model of a probabilistic analogue in-memory computing processor to claim speedups of over 1000x versus data-center-grade hardware.

- **Technical:** Massively parallel models with low arithmetic intensity are argued to be mismatched with GPUs that depend on HBM interfaces, causing sequential execution constraints.
- **Technical:** Eliminating the HBM interface and computing directly within on-chip memory is presented as the route to much faster execution of the energy-based model.
- **Business:** The report claims the approach could execute the model well over 1000x faster than data-center-grade hardware, a claim based on a transaction-level model rather than silicon.

Sources: [arXiv API](<https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11288>)
